# Frontend audit — confirmed findings

Produced in session 26 by a 24-agent sweep over the 59 screens that existed then — there are **79**
now, and the twenty added since had a sweep of their own in session 28, recorded at the end of this
file. The first sweep's method: twelve agents read a group of five
against the backend module each `api.*` call reaches, and twelve more tried to **refute** what the
first twelve found. **204 findings judged, 174 confirmed, 30 refuted.**

## How to read this

- Every entry below survived an adversarial pass whose instruction was to default to REFUTED when
  uncertain, and whose first step was to check the quoted code exists verbatim. One finding was
  dropped for a fabricated quotation.
- **A confirmed finding is still a claim.** Verify the quotation before acting on it — that is this
  project’s recorded doctrine and it caught a real fabrication in an earlier session.
- `Closed` first marked the ones fixed in session 26, parts 36–38. Each was re-verified by hand
  before being acted on, and the ones with visible behaviour were reproduced in a browser first —
  both before the change and after it. Findings closed later say on their own status line how they
  were checked, because the later ones were not all checked that way.
- Nothing here authorises a new table, column, permission, module key or limit key. The catalogue is
  fixed at 109 permissions / 11 roles / 20 module keys / 8 limit keys / 64 domain tables (SRS §29,
  §35), and `docs/SRS-extracted.md` remains the sole source of truth.

**174 closed, 0 open**, re-counted from the status lines on 2026-09-10. Later in session 28 the last 80 were closed — 43 **Poor** and 37 **Minor** — in one pass: 56 fixed, 18 found already fixed in the tree with only the status line left behind, and 6 half-fixed and finished. Those 80 were checked a different way from the 94 before them, and the status line of each says how: every change was reviewed against the backend it calls by a second, read-only reviewer whose findings were re-checked by hand before acting, the whole frontend type-checks and lints clean, and `verify-frontend.js` passes — but **none was reproduced in a browser**, because signing in needs a password this session does not enter. (Earlier in session 28 this header read *"94 closed, 80 open"*, and before that *"84 closed, 90 open"*, when ten findings closed in sessions 27 and 28 had been marked closed on the finding and nowhere else.)

**The twenty screens added since this sweep have now had one of their own** — see *Session 28: the screens this sweep never saw* at the end of this file.

## Broken — a user meets an error, a dead end, or a wrong answer

17 finding(s) — 17 closed, 0 open.

### Login routes every non-platform user to /school, so Parents land on the school-admin dashboard and can never reach the /parent portal that exists. — `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `ux` · line 51
- **What a user sees:** A parent signing in lands on 'School dashboard — Welcome back, <name>' with the school's plan name, subscription state and plan limits ((school)/school/page.tsx renders these from the entitlement snapshot with no API call, so nothing 403s to explain it), an empty shortcut list (every entry is filtered by can()), and a sidebar filtered to nothing. /parent is unreachable: the only href to it is inside PARENT_NAV, which only renders once you are already at /parent.
- **Evidence:** login/page.tsx:51 is verbatim `router.replace(result.profile.tenant.isPlatform ? '/super-admin' : '/school');`. permissions.js:400 `[ROLES.PARENT]: [` lists 'parents.dashboard.view', 'students.self.view', … and contains no 'school.dashboard.view' (defined at permissions.js:75, granted at :264/:295/:328/:346/:363/:377). permissions.js:383 `[ROLES.STUDENT]: [` likewise omits it. (parent)/parent/layout.tsx and (student)/student/layout.tsx exist and render AppShell with PARENT_NAV / STUDENT_NAV (nav.ts). shell.tsx:156 is the only guard: `if (!loading && !profile) router.replace('/login');` — it checks for a session, not a surface — and shell.tsx builds `const home = profile.tenant.isPlatform ? '/super-admin' : '/school';`. app/page.tsx:116 repeats the same two-way rule. auth.service.js publicUser() does emit `out.role = { id, slug, name, isPlatformRole, isSchoolRole }` and authenticate.js:118 loads `include: [{ model: db.Role, as: 'role', attributes: ['id', 'slug', 'name', 'is_platform_role', 'is_school_role'] }]`, so the data needed to route correctly is present.
- **Fix:** Route by capability rather than by isPlatform: isPlatform -> /super-admin; 'parents.dashboard.view' -> /parent; 'teachers.dashboard.view' -> /teacher; 'school.dashboard.view' -> /school; 'students.self.view' -> /student. Order matters because a Teacher holds both 'school.dashboard.view' and 'teachers.dashboard.view'. Uses only existing catalogue keys — no new permission, table or limit.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote at line 51 is exact. Read config/permissions.js: the PARENT block at :400 and STUDENT block at :383 genuinely lack 'school.dashboard.view'; grep confirms it is granted only at 264/295/328/346/363/377. Parent accounts are creatable through the product (parents.service.js create() does db.User.create with the parent role), so the impact is reachable today. One caveat that does not change the verdict: no code path creates a STUDENT user account — students.service.js contains no db.User.create and users.routes.js has no POST — so the student half of the summary is currently hypothetical; the parent half alone establishes the defect.

### The forced-password-change screen repeats the login screen's routing bug, and it is the screen every Parent and Principal account is forced through on first sign-in. — `src/app/(auth)/change-password/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `ux` · line 59
- **What a user sees:** Every parent account a school creates reaches /change-password on first sign-in, changes its password successfully, and is redirected to a school-administration dashboard it holds no permission to use, instead of /parent.
- **Evidence:** change-password/page.tsx:59 is verbatim `router.replace(profile.tenant.isPlatform ? '/super-admin' : '/school');`. A grep of the whole backend for `must_change_password: true` returns exactly three writers: database/seeders/04-super-admin.js:89, modules/principals/principals.service.js:242 (inside db.User.create for a new Principal) and modules/parents/parents.service.js:261 (inside db.User.create for a new Parent). So every parent and every principal created through the product is sent to this screen on first sign-in and then to /school.
- **Fix:** Use the same capability-based landing-route helper as the login screen; extract it once (e.g. into lib/nav.ts) so the two screens cannot drift.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Line 59 matches character for character. I ran the grep myself: `modules/parents/parents.service.js:261: must_change_password: true,` and `modules/principals/principals.service.js:242: must_change_password: true,` both exist, inside the User.create of each service's create(). Same permission evidence as the login finding applies.

### Signing in rotates the server's CSRF cookie but the client keeps the token it cached before login, so /auth/refresh and /auth/logout are both refused with 403 for the rest of the session. — `src/lib/apiClient.ts`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `session` · line 183
- **What a user sees:** Cookie holds the token publishSession minted; the cached header holds the one fetched from /csrf-token just before login. About fifteen minutes in, the first 401 triggers POST /auth/refresh, which is refused 403 CSRF_TOKEN_INVALID; refreshAccessToken() returns false and the user is thrown back to /login mid-task. 'Sign out' fails the same way: the UI clears, but authService.logout() never runs, the refresh_token_hash is not cleared, and the httpOnly refresh cookie stays valid. The same desync re-forms after every page reload, because the bootstrap refresh itself rotates the cookie while the cache keeps the value it just fetched.
- **Evidence:** auth.controller.js publishSession, lines 113-114: `const csrfToken = config.security.csrfEnabled ? issueCsrfToken(res) : null;` / `if (csrfToken) body.csrfToken = csrfToken;`. csrf.js:72-76 issueCsrfToken mints a fresh value and re-sets the cookie (same name, path '/') on every call. env.js:192 `csrfEnabled: bool('CSRF_ENABLED', !isTest),` — on outside tests. The client never adopts the rotated value: apiClient.ts:183 `if (csrfToken) return csrfToken;` caches indefinitely, apiClient.ts:196 `csrfToken = body.data?.token ?? null;` is the only writer, and a grep for 'csrf' across frontend/src returns only apiClient.ts plus auth.tsx:22/113 (the clearCsrfToken import and its call inside clear()). auth.tsx:150 reads only `result.accessToken`; LoginResult (auth.tsx:63-66) declares only `accessToken` and `user?`. The two guarded routes are auth.routes.js:74 `requireCsrfToken(),` on POST /refresh and auth.routes.js:116 `protectedRoutes.post('/logout', requireCsrfToken(), asyncHandler(controller.logout));`, and csrf.js:136 refuses a mismatch: `if (!cookieToken || !headerToken || !secretsMatch(cookieToken, headerToken)) {`. apiClient.ts:236 sends the stale cached value on refresh; auth.tsx:209 `if (!(error instanceof ApiError)) throw error;` swallows the 403 on logout. clear() (the only caller of clearCsrfToken) runs on logout and session-loss, never after a successful login.
- **Fix:** Export setCsrfToken(token) from lib/apiClient.ts and call it with the csrfToken the server returns, in auth.tsx login() and changePassword() and in refreshAccessToken(); add `csrfToken?: string` to LoginResult. The one-line alternative is to call clearCsrfToken() after any response that rotates the session.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every cited line matched exactly — I grepped auth.controller.js and got `113:  const csrfToken = config.security.csrfEnabled ? issueCsrfToken(res) : null;` / `114:  if (csrfToken) body.csrfToken = csrfToken;`, and auth.routes.js gave `74:  requireCsrfToken(),` and `116:protectedRoutes.post('/logout', requireCsrfToken(), ...)`. issueCsrfToken re-issues unconditionally with the same cookie name and path, so it overwrites. My own grep of frontend/src for 'csrf' returned no reader of any response body's csrfToken. Both origins are localhost, so the cookie is shared across the two ports and SameSite=lax permits the fetch — the desync is real, not blocked by cookie policy. Note the blast radius is exactly the two guarded routes: ordinary mutations (POST /students etc.) are unaffected, since csrf.js is deliberately scoped.

### A token mangled by an email client returns a 422 whose only message is attached to the field `token`, which this page deliberately does not render, and the banner is suppressed — so the submit produces no visible output at all. — `src/app/(auth)/reset-password/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 57
- **What a user sees:** The user clicks 'Set new password', the button flickers through 'Saving…', and nothing happens — no banner, no field message, no success. The server's 'This link is not valid.' is discarded, and the only recovery is guessing that the link is broken.
- **Evidence:** reset-password/page.tsx:52-57 is verbatim `setFieldErrors(caught.fieldErrors());` followed by the comment and `setError(caught.details.length ? null : caught.message);`. The page renders exactly two error sinks, at lines 121 and 131: `error={fieldErrors.password}` and `error={fieldErrors.confirmation}`; there is no input with id 'token', by design per the header at lines 6-8. Server side, auth.validation.js:134-137 is `resetPassword: Joi.object({ token: singleUseToken, password: newPassword })` and singleUseToken (auth.validation.js:63-70) is `.min(20).max(200).pattern(/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/)` with `.messages({ 'string.pattern.base': 'This link is not valid.' })`. validate.js:90-95 keys the failure to the field name: `details.push({ field: item.path.join('.'), location: container, message: item.message, type: item.type });` — so details = [{ field: 'token', … }], fieldErrors() returns { token: … } which nothing renders, details.length is truthy, and setError(null) suppresses the banner. The page's guard at line 70 `if (!token) {` catches only an empty token, so the exact case its own copy names at lines 75-76 ('broken across two lines by an email client') produces a non-empty partial token that reaches this silent path.
- **Fix:** Fall back to the banner when a field error has no matching input, e.g. setError(fe.token ?? caught.formErrors()[0] ?? ((fe.password || fe.confirmation) ? null : caught.message)). ApiError.formErrors() already exists at apiClient.ts:138 for this class of message and no auth screen calls it.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quotes verified verbatim in all three files, including validate.js:90-95's details.push and auth.validation.js's singleUseToken with its min/max/pattern. The reset-password schema does use singleUseToken for `token`. Note the reviewer's line numbers for the two error sinks (113-132) are approximate — they are at 121 and 131 — but the substance holds: there is no `token` sink on the page. This is the same failure mode apiClient.ts:118-123 already documents for the '' key ('the message disappeared completely and a rejected submit looked like nothing had happened'), reached here through a named field instead.

### `ChildLink.is_primary` does not exist on the payload — the `parent_students` column is `is_primary_guardian`, so the "Primary contact" column renders "no" for every child. — `frontend/src/app/(parent)/parent/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 42
- **What a user sees:** Every row of the one screen a parent has says "no" under "Primary contact", including the child the office marked as first contact. `row.is_primary` is `undefined` on every row, so the branch can never take the 'yes' arm.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:42 `is_primary: boolean;` and page.tsx:132 `cell: (row) => (row.is_primary ? 'yes' : <span className="text-muted-soft">no</span>),`. The payload is raw model rows: parents.controller.js:68 `async function dashboard(req, res) {` → `return ApiResponse.ok(res, data);`, parents.service.js:530 `children: links,` where `links = await db.ParentStudent.findAll({...})`. models/people.js:170 `is_primary_guardian: { type: DataTypes.BOOLEAN, allowNull: false, defaultValue: false },` is the only such column on ParentStudent.
- **Fix:** Rename the field to `is_primary_guardian: boolean;` and read `row.is_primary_guardian`, matching (school)/school/parents/new/page.tsx:124.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both quotes are verbatim at lines 42 and 132. `grep -n is_primary src/models/people.js` returns exactly one hit — line 170 `is_primary_guardian:` — and the ParentStudent definition (people.js:160-179) declares only `id, school_id, parent_id, student_id, relation, is_primary_guardian`. parents.controller.js:68 and parents.service.js:530 are exactly where cited, and nothing between the model and `res.json` renames the column. `grep -rn is_primary frontend/src` shows this file is the only one using the short name; every other site uses `is_primary_guardian`.

### The add-on name links to `/super-admin/addons/{id}`, a route that does not exist, so the screen's only affordance lands on the not-found page and FR-SUB-009's activate/deactivate and price set are unreachable from the UI. — `frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/addons/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `dead-link` · line 148
- **What a user sees:** A Super Admin clicking the add-on name gets (platform)/not-found.tsx — whose own header says it is "the answer to a mistyped URL" — for a link the product rendered; the `is_active` switch the header calls "the one column an operator comes here to *change*" has no UI path at all.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:148 `<a href={`/super-admin/addons/${row.id}`} className="font-medium underline underline-offset-2">`. `find src/app -type d | grep '\['` returns nothing (no dynamic segments anywhere in the app) and `src/app/(platform)/super-admin/addons/` contains only `page.tsx`. next.config.mjs declares no rewrites. Backend routes exist and are UI-orphaned: addons.routes.js:90 and :99 `router.post('/:id/activate' | '/:id/deactivate', ...)`, :116 `router.put('/:id/prices', ...)`; `grep -rn "addons/" frontend/src` finds only this href and a header comment.
- **Fix:** Build `(platform)/super-admin/addons/[id]/page.tsx`, or drop the anchor and put an activate/deactivate control inline on the row (POST /addons/:id/activate|deactivate, confirm on deactivate) until the detail screen exists.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The href is verbatim at line 148. Directory listing of the addons folder shows one file (`page.tsx`, 12879 bytes); a `find src/app -type d | grep -E "\["` produced no output at all, so there is no `[id]` segment and no catch-all; next.config.mjs contains only `reactStrictMode` and `poweredByHeader` — no rewrites. `grep -n "router.post|router.put|router.patch" addons.routes.js` gives 79/90/99/116, matching the cited routes, and no frontend file calls any of them.

### Switching to the Subscriptions tab while another report is on screen renders the stale payload through the SubscriptionReport cast and throws Object.entries(undefined) during render. — `(platform)/super-admin/reports/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 649
- **What a user sees:** Run the Students (or Fees, Exams, Teachers) report, then click the Subscriptions tab: one render happens with spec.type==='subscription', loading===false and `report` still the previous payload, so Object.entries(undefined) throws mid-render. There is no error.tsx or global-error.tsx anywhere under frontend/src/app, so the crash reaches Next's default handler and the page is lost until reload.
- **Evidence:** The cast keys off the selected spec, not the payload (page.tsx:451-452): `const subscription =\n    spec.type === 'subscription' ? (report as unknown as SubscriptionReport | null) : null;`. The render then dereferences subscription-only fields: line 645 `{Object.entries(subscription.by_state).map(([state, count]) => (` and line 659 `rows={subscription.by_plan}`. The tab handler changes only tab state (483-485): `setSelected(entry.type);` / `setParams({});`. `report` is cleared only inside the read effect (339-341 `if (!allowed || missing.length > 0) { setReport(null); setLoading(false);`), which is a useEffect and therefore runs after that render commits.
- **Fix:** Branch on the payload's own discriminator instead of the selected tab: `const subscription = report && report.type === 'subscription' ? ... : null`, and clear `report` in the tab onClick.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every quote is verbatim (a uniform ~4-line offset from the numbers given). reports.service.js:159-171 returns the student payload with keys `type, school, window, total, by_status, by_gender, by_class` — no by_state, no by_plan — while by_state/by_plan appear only in subscriptions() at reports.service.js:390-402. constants.js:655 `SUBSCRIPTION: 'subscription',` makes the proposed discriminator fix correct. The only escape from the throw is a stale payload whose `total` is exactly 0, which hits the EmptyNotice at 635-636.

### The "Add section" button points at a route that does not exist, so it 404s. — `(school)/school/classes/sections/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `dead-link` · line 155
- **What a user sees:** A Principal on the Sections screen clicks the primary blue "Add section" button and lands on Next's 404 page, losing the class they had selected.
- **Evidence:** sections/page.tsx:153-159 renders `<a href={`/school/classes/${classId}/sections/new`} className="btn btn-primary">Add section</a>`. No such route exists.
- **Fix:** Either build `(school)/school/classes/[id]/sections/new/page.tsx` posting to `POST /classes/{id}/sections`, or drop the button until that screen exists.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote is verbatim at 153-159. Stronger than claimed: `find src/app -type d -name "*[[]*"` returns NOTHING — the app router has no dynamic segment anywhere in the whole frontend, so `/school/classes/7/sections/new` cannot resolve. next.config.mjs declares only `reactStrictMode: true` and `poweredByHeader: false` — no rewrites (its own comment: "there is no rewrite proxying `/api` into Express"). Backend side checks out: classes.routes.js has `router.post('/:id/sections', requirePermission('classes.manage'), validate({ params: schemas.idParam, body: schemas.createSection }), ...)`, and classes.validation.js:67-77 is exactly as described, ending `class_id: forbiddenField('"class_id" is the path parameter')`.

### The category filter state leaks across the Income/Expenses tabs, so switching tabs with a filter on sends an off-enum value and 422s the whole ledger. — `(school)/school/finance/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `ux` · line 327
- **What a user sees:** Filter Income by Fees, click Expenses: the table is replaced by a red validation-error panel whose Try again re-sends the same query and fails identically. `search` and `page` leak the same way.
- **Evidence:** finance/page.tsx:326-328 is verbatim `<TabPanel tabKey={active}>` / `<LedgerPanel kind={active === 'expenses' ? 'expenses' : 'incomes'} />`. components/tabs.tsx:77-83 shows TabPanel is a plain `<div role="tabpanel" id={`panel-${tabKey}`}>` with no key on its children, so LedgerPanel is the same element type in the same position across a tab switch and its `const [category, setCategory] = useState('');` (line 99) survives. The enums are disjoint (constants.js:480-483 salaries/other_expenses; 485-488 fees/other_income) and finance.validation.js:94 pins the expense filter to `Joi.string().valid(...Object.values(EXPENSE_CATEGORIES))`; ApiError.js:66-68 makes that a 422 VALIDATION_ERROR, which is absent from EXPLAINED_CODES (useCollection.ts:75-100), so useCollection.ts:167-168 takes `setError(caught.message)` and finance/page.tsx:204-205 replaces the table with ErrorNotice.
- **Fix:** Give the panel a key so it remounts per ledger (`<LedgerPanel key={active} …/>`), or lift page/search/category into FinanceScreen and reset them in setActive.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote is exact. tabs.tsx:79 is `<div role="tabpanel" id={`panel-${tabKey}`} aria-labelledby={`tab-${tabKey}`}>` — no key, so React preserves LedgerPanel state; finance.validation.js:94 really is `  category: Joi.string().valid(...Object.values(EXPENSE_CATEGORIES)),`. One correction to the impact: the escape is NOT a page reload — the filter row (finance/page.tsx:150-200) renders above the error branch, so re-selecting 'Any category' recovers. That makes the recovery cheaper than claimed, but the failure is real.

### The primary Record income / Record expense buttons point at routes that do not exist, so both land on the 404 page. — `(school)/school/finance/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `dead-link` · line 194
- **What a user sees:** An accountant or principal with finance.manage clicks the only call-to-action on the Finance screen and gets a 404; §18 income/expense entry is unreachable from the UI.
- **Evidence:** finance/page.tsx:192-198 is verbatim `{can('finance.manage') ? (` / `<a href={`/school/finance/${kind}/new`} …>`. A full directory listing of frontend/src/app shows `(school)/school/finance` with no child directories at all — no incomes/new, no expenses/new, and no dynamic or catch-all segment anywhere under app. next.config.mjs declares only `reactStrictMode` and `poweredByHeader: false` — no rewrites. app/not-found.tsx exists and is what renders. The endpoints are real and permissioned: finance.routes.js:107-113 mounts POST /incomes and :139-145 POST /expenses, both behind `requirePermission('finance.manage')`.
- **Fix:** Build (school)/school/finance/incomes/new and .../expenses/new against POST /finance/incomes and /finance/expenses, or remove the buttons until they exist.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `find . -type d` under frontend/src/app returns `./(school)/school/finance` with no children, while sibling screens list `./(school)/school/fees/structures/new` etc. The route genuinely does not exist. Minor imprecision only: line 107 is POST /incomes and 139 is POST /expenses — the finding names them in the opposite order.

### The search box and status select hard-code bg-white next to text-ink, which is near-white in dark mode — white text on a white field. — `(school)/school/exams/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 147
- **What a user sees:** Any user on a system in dark mode sees a white input and a white select whose text is #f1f3f7 — effectively invisible.
- **Evidence:** exams/page.tsx:146-148 is verbatim `const CONTROL_CLASS =` / `'rounded-md border border-border-strong bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink outline-none ' +` / `'focus:border-teal';`, applied at :349 `className={`w-full max-w-sm ${CONTROL_CLASS}`}` and :363 `className={CONTROL_CLASS}`. globals.css:203 and :252 both set `--ink: var(--slate-100);` (dark), and :39 defines `--slate-100: #f1f3f7;` — near-white on Tailwind's literal #fff. The dark block is reached without an explicit choice: globals.css:201-202 is `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme="light"]) {`, and components/theme.tsx:49 removes data-theme entirely for the 'system' setting. Grepping `white` in globals.css finds only `white-space`, so --color-white is not redefined.
- **Fix:** Use field-input / field-select (background: var(--surface-1), globals.css:601) or the shared SearchInput/SelectField.
- **Why it survived refutation:** theme.tsx:49 `root.removeAttribute('data-theme');` proves the prefers-color-scheme branch is live for default users, and globals.css:39 `--slate-100: #f1f3f7;` against Tailwind's unmodified #fff is ~1.07:1 contrast. Only overstatement: `grep -rln bg-white` returns 13 files total (including exams and fees themselves), not 13 *other* screens.

### The ledger status select hard-codes bg-white with text-ink, rendering white-on-white in dark mode. — `(school)/school/fees/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 217
- **What a user sees:** In dark mode the only filter on the Ledger tab is an unreadable white box; the user cannot see whether they are viewing Unpaid, Paid or Waived.
- **Evidence:** fees/page.tsx:213-218 is verbatim, including line 217 `          className="rounded-md border border-border-strong bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink"`. Same token facts as the exams finding: globals.css:252 `--ink: var(--slate-100);` under `:root[data-theme="dark"]` (line 251) and :203 under the prefers-color-scheme block opened at :201-202, with --slate-100 = #f1f3f7 at globals.css:39.
- **Fix:** Replace with SelectField from components/form.tsx, which applies .field-select (background: var(--surface-1)).
- **Why it survived refutation:** Line 217 of fees/page.tsx matches the quoted class string character for character, and .field-select/.field-input set `background: var(--surface-1);` at globals.css:601 while bg-white stays #fff in both themes.

### `fine_paid` is typed and read as a boolean, but the column is DECIMAL(14,2) money, so any part-payment reports the whole fine as "paid". — `(school)/school/library/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 219
- **What a user sees:** A 10.00 fine with 4.00 paid arrives as the number 4, which is truthy, so the cell says "paid" and the 6.00 still owed is invisible — worst in the "Only unpaid fines" list, whose server filter (library.service.js:346-358, `{ fine_waived: false, fine_amount: { [Op.gt]: paid } }`) returns exactly the rows where fine_paid is between 0 and fine_amount.
- **Evidence:** library/page.tsx:63 `  fine_paid: boolean;` and :217-220 `const amount = Number(row.fine_amount ?? 0); if (!amount) …; if (row.fine_paid) return <span className="text-muted-soft">paid</span>;`. backend/src/models/other.js:404-406 `fine_amount: money({ defaultValue: 0 }), fine_paid: money({ defaultValue: 0 }), fine_waived: { type: DataTypes.BOOLEAN, … }`; models/columns.js:60-61 `const money = (options = {}) => ({ type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(14, 2),`; config/database.js:59 `decimalNumbers: true,`.
- **Fix:** Type `fine_amount: number; fine_paid: number; fine_outstanding: number;` and render the balance the service already sends — presentTransaction (library.service.js:133-146) returns `fine_outstanding: money.clampNonNegative(money.subtract(row.fine_amount || 0, row.fine_paid || 0))` — showing "paid" only when it is 0.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every quote is verbatim and correctly located. library/page.tsx:63 is `  fine_paid: boolean;`; other.js:404-405 are both `money({ defaultValue: 0 })`; columns.js:60-61 are `const money = (options = {}) => ({` / `  type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(14, 2),`. settleFine's only guard is library.service.js:570 `if (payload.fine_paid !== undefined && Number(payload.fine_paid) > amount) {`, so a partial payment is legal, and Joi accepts a sum (library.validation.js:44 `const moneyField = Joi.number().min(0).max(9999999999.99).precision(2);`). presentTransaction spreads `row.toJSON()`, so the number reaches the cell.

### `!entitlements` is treated as "this school has no plan", but the API returns null only for a caller with no school in scope, and login routes Organization Admins to this URL. — `(school)/school/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 48
- **What a user sees:** An Organization Admin (and a Super Admin who opens /school) sees only "No subscription attached" — no limits, module list or shortcuts, since all of them sit inside the `entitlements` branch — and the copy blames a subscription that is not the problem.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:48-54 `{!entitlements ? (… <p …>No subscription attached</p> <p …>This school has no active plan yet. A platform administrator can subscribe it from the Subscriptions screen.</p>`. backend/src/modules/auth/auth.service.js:844-850 `async function callerEntitlements(req) { … if (!req.tenant || req.tenant.isPlatform || !req.tenant.schoolId) return null;`. An unsubscribed school gets an object instead: entitlementService.js:262-272 `unsubscribedSnapshot()` returns `{ …, subscription: null, plan: null, modules: emptyModules(), limits: emptyLimits(), … }`, already handled at page.tsx:63.
- **Fix:** Branch on the flag the provider already exposes: lib/entitlements.tsx:110 `const isSubscriptionScoped = entitlements !== null;`, returned in the context value at :131. Say the dashboard needs one school in scope when it is false, and keep the "no plan yet" copy for the snapshot-with-`plan === null` case at :63-69.
- **Why it survived refutation:** auth.service.js:850 is exactly `  if (!req.tenant || req.tenant.isPlatform || !req.tenant.schoolId) return null;`, and resolveTenant.js:149-155 gives an organization caller `{ isPlatform: false, organizationId, schoolId: req.user.school_id || null, level: 'organization', roleSlug }`. The role reaches this screen: permissions.js:264 grants `'school.dashboard.view'` inside the `[ROLES.ORGANIZATION_ADMIN]` block (256-285), app/(auth)/login/page.tsx:51 does `router.replace(result.profile.tenant.isPlatform ? '/super-admin' : '/school');`, and app/(school)/school/layout.tsx is only `<AppShell nav={SCHOOL_NAV}>` with no per-page guard. entitlements.tsx:88 even documents the state: "Null for a platform or organization caller".

### The three filter selects hardcode `bg-white` while taking their text colour from the `--ink` token, so in dark mode they render near-white text on a white box. — `frontend/src/app/(school)/school/subjects/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 150
- **What a user sees:** On a dark OS theme or an explicit dark choice, the selected value in each of the three filter dropdowns is #f1f3f7 on #ffffff (~1.03:1), so the user cannot read what the list is currently filtered by.
- **Evidence:** subjects/page.tsx:149-151 reads exactly `const CONTROL_CLASS =` / `'rounded-md border border-border-strong bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink outline-none ' +` / `'focus:border-teal';`, applied at lines 317, 333 and 349. globals.css:201-203 is `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {` / `:root:not([data-theme="light"]) {` / `--ink: var(--slate-100);`, repeated at :root[data-theme="dark"] (251-252), and globals.css:301 is `--color-ink: var(--ink);` inside `@theme inline`. globals.css:39 is `--slate-100: #f1f3f7;` — near-white on #fff. The dark theme is genuinely reachable: components/theme.tsx:51 does `root.setAttribute('data-theme', choice);`.
- **Fix:** Give the three selects `className="field-select"` or use `SelectField`; both resolve background through `--surface-1`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote is verbatim at 149-151. Decisive corroboration: the two sibling screens do it correctly — students/page.tsx:362 and teachers/page.tsx:321 are both `className="field-input mt-0"`, and `grep -n bg-white` across the three list files matches only subjects/page.tsx:150.

### `subjects` is typed as a NamedRow with a top-level `name`, but the API sends `teacher_subjects` join rows whose name is one level deeper at `row.subject.name`, so every subject chip renders empty. — `(teacher)/teacher/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `envelope` · line 28
- **What a user sees:** The "My subjects" panel renders one blank teal pill per assignment: `row.name` and `row.code` are both undefined, so the `<li>` shows only its padding. A teacher assigned three subjects sees three unlabelled capsules.
- **Evidence:** Frontend lines 19-31 are verbatim as quoted (`interface NamedRow { id; name; code?; }`, `subjects: NamedRow[];`) and line 122 is `{row.name}` inside the pill `<li>`. Backend `teachers.service.js:343-346` is `db.TeacherSubject.findAll({ where: { teacher_id: teacher.id, school_id: teacher.school_id }, include: [ { model: db.Subject, as: 'subject', attributes: ['id', 'name', 'code', 'type'] },` and the controller is `async function dashboard(req, res) { const data = await service.dashboard(req); return ApiResponse.ok(res, data); }`, so `data.subjects` is the join row set. `models/academic.js:191-212` defines TeacherSubject with only `id, school_id, teacher_id, subject_id, class_id, section_id, is_primary, is_active` — no `name`, no `code`. `teachers.service.js:393` reads `subjects.map((r) => Number(r.subject_id))`, which only works on join rows.
- **Fix:** Type the row as `{ id: number; subject: { id: number; name: string; code: string | null } | null; class?: {...} | null; section?: {...} | null }` and render `row.subject?.name`. `classTeacherOf` and `sectionTeacherOf` really do carry a top-level `name` (`attributes: ['id','name','academic_session_id','is_active']` / `['id','name','class_id','is_active']`), so only the `subjects` branch changes.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both halves check out verbatim. The include is `{ model: db.Subject, as: 'subject', attributes: ['id', 'name', 'code', 'type'] }` at teachers.service.js:346 — the name lives on the nested `subject`, and the TeacherSubject model block (academic.js:191-212) contains no `name` column at all.

### The "Held" column reads `exam.start_date`, but the `exam` include selects only `['id','name','exam_type']`, so the field never arrives and the whole column is permanently an em-dash. — `(student)/student/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 92
- **What a user sees:** Every row of the student's results table shows "—" under "Held"; the column consumes width and conveys nothing, and a student cannot tell which exam period a result belongs to.
- **Evidence:** Line 61 is verbatim `exam?: { id: number; name: string; exam_type: string | null; start_date: string | null };` and line 92 is verbatim `{ key: 'date', header: 'Held', cell: (row) => row.exam?.start_date?.slice(0, 10) ?? <span className="text-muted-soft">—</span> },`. `exams.service.js:1571` is `{ model: db.Exam, as: 'exam', attributes: ['id', 'name', 'exam_type'] },` inside `myResults`'s `paginateQuery`. `exams.controller.js:210-214` does `return ApiResponse.paginated(res, result, pagination);` with no `present()`, so the wire shape is the row as queried. `models/exams.js:108` has `start_date: { type: DataTypes.DATEONLY, allowNull: true },` — the column exists but is outside the attributes allow-list.
- **Fix:** Add `'start_date'` to the `exam` include's attributes in `myResults`, or drop the column and the field from the interface.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Grep pins the include to one line: `1571:        { model: db.Exam, as: 'exam', attributes: ['id', 'name', 'exam_type'] },` — no `start_date`, and no `present()` in the controller could add it.

## Wrong — renders or behaves incorrectly without erroring

42 finding(s) — **42 closed, 0 open.**

### The failure state tells the user to 'request a new link from your profile', but no profile screen exists and nothing in the frontend ever calls POST /auth/resend-verification. — `src/app/(auth)/verify-email/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `dead-link` · line 99
- **What a user sees:** A user whose verification link expired follows the instruction, signs in, and finds no profile page and no resend control anywhere in the product. They are left with an unverified address and no self-service route to fix it.
- **Evidence:** verify-email/page.tsx:97-100 is verbatim: `<p className="text-sm text-muted">` / 'Verification links expire, and each one can be used only once. Sign in and request a new' / 'link from your profile.' The backend endpoint exists — auth.routes.js mounts `protectedRoutes.post('/resend-verification', authLimiter, validate({ body: schemas.resendVerification }), asyncHandler(controller.resendVerification));` — but my grep of frontend/src for 'resend' returns only two comment lines in (platform)/super-admin/principals/page.tsx (:26 and :166). No component calls it. A find over src/app for a page.tsx matching profile|account|settings returns only (platform)/super-admin/settings/page.tsx, which contains no `api.` call at all.
- **Fix:** Either build the resend control (a button on a profile/account screen calling api.post('/auth/resend-verification')) and point this copy at it, or change the copy to say what is true — that they must ask an administrator to resend the confirmation email.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Copy quoted exactly at 97-100. I ran the grep: 'resend' appears in frontend/src only at principals/page.tsx:26 and :166, both inside comments. The find for profile/account/settings pages returned only super-admin/settings/page.tsx, and grepping it for 'api.' returned nothing. The backend route is mounted but has no caller, so the instruction the copy gives cannot be followed.

### The comment asserts that no code path sets must_change_password except the Super Admin seeder; two service modules do. — `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 39
- **What a user sees:** No runtime effect. It misdirects the next maintainer: it presents /change-password as a one-time bootstrap screen for a fresh deployment, when every Principal and every Parent account created through the product passes through it on first sign-in — which is why the routing bug on line 51 matters.
- **Evidence:** login/page.tsx:38-45 is verbatim, including 'and, despite what this comment used to say, on nobody else: no code path sets it when an administrator types a user's initial password.' A grep of the whole backend for `must_change_password: true` returns three writers: 04-super-admin.js:89, principals.service.js:242 and parents.service.js:261. principals.service.js:14-17 states the intent outright: '**`must_change_password`** — always true. §9.3 has the Super Admin type the Principal's password into a form, so somebody other than the account holder knows it'. The same false claim sits in the backend at authenticate.js:206-207 ('That seeder is the **only** writer that sets it. This paragraph used to add…'), which is equally wrong.
- **Fix:** Delete the 'and, despite what this comment used to say, on nobody else' clause and name the three writers: the seeder, principals.service.js:242 and parents.service.js:261.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The comment is verbatim at 38-45, and the specific clause it contradicts is exactly the case principals.service.js implements: line 242 sets the flag on the account whose initial password the Super Admin typed. I read principals.service.js:14-17 and it says '**`must_change_password`** — always true'. The stale backend twin at authenticate.js:206-207 is real too — I read it, and it even claims 'grepping the column across `src/` finds the seeder setting `true`… nothing else', which my own grep disproves.

### The header claims the bootstrap seeder is the only writer of must_change_password; principals.service.js and parents.service.js both set it deliberately. — `src/app/(auth)/change-password/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 9
- **What a user sees:** No runtime effect, but the file describes itself as a new-deployment bootstrap screen when it is on the first-login path of every principal and every parent account. A maintainer trusting it would under-test the screen and miss that its line 59 redirect sends parents to the wrong surface.
- **Evidence:** change-password/page.tsx:8-11 is verbatim, including '(it is the only writer that does; §9.3 captures a Password field but requires no forced change)'. Contradicted by principals.service.js:242 `must_change_password: true,` and parents.service.js:261 `must_change_password: true,`.
- **Fix:** Correct the parenthetical to name all three writers (04-super-admin.js:89, principals.service.js:242, parents.service.js:261).
- **Why it survived refutation:** The header text is verbatim at lines 8-11 and 'it is the only writer that does' is false by my own grep — three writers exist. One correction to the reviewer's reasoning, which does not change the verdict: its second charge is wrong. '§9.3 captures a Password field but requires no forced change' is a claim about the SRS, and it is accurate — principals.service.js:16-17 itself says the forced change is 'Not an SRS requirement, and not presented as one', and authenticate.js:210-212 says '§9.3 lists Password among the fields principal creation captures and says nothing about forcing a change afterwards'. Only the 'only writer' clause should be corrected; the §9.3 sentence should be left alone.

### The credit balance is interpolated raw instead of going through lib/money.ts, so cents are dropped — DECIMAL arrives as a JS number, not the string the interface claims. — `frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/invoices/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 606
- **What a user sees:** The checkbox that decides whether the invoice draws down credit reads "(USD 0)" for a zero balance and "(USD 1200.5)" for 1,200.50 — an operator cannot tell five cents from fifty on the figure that changes what the invoice is raised for.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:606 `{selected ? ` (${selected.currency} ${selected.credit_balance})` : ''}` with page.tsx:120 `credit_balance: string;`. models/subscription.js:411 `credit_balance: money({ defaultValue: 0, comment: 'Unapplied proration credit (SRS §12.3)' }),`; models/columns.js `const money = (options = {}) => ({ type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(14, 2),`; config/database.js `dialectOptions: { // Return DECIMAL as string-free numbers where safe; money is handled via the money util.` + `decimalNumbers: true,` under `dialect: 'mysql'`. subscriptions.controller.js `present()` returns `{ ...json, standing: service.standing(subscription) }` — no reformatting. `formatCodeWithAmount(currency, value)` exists in lib/money.ts for exactly this.
- **Fix:** Render `formatCodeWithAmount(selected.currency, selected.credit_balance)` from @/lib/money and type the field `credit_balance: number;`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Line 606 and line 120 are verbatim. subscription.js:411 is the cited `money()` column, columns.js defines `money` as `DECIMAL(14, 2)`, and database.js sets `decimalNumbers: true` on the mysql dialect — matching the session fact that every DECIMAL arrives as a JS number. `present()` in subscriptions.controller.js only adds `standing` and per-override `is_effective`, so the raw number reaches the template literal; `${1200.5}` is "1200.5". lib/money.ts:68-75 `formatCodeWithAmount` exists and returns `USD 1,200.00`.

### The review dialog reports that a screenshot is attached but gives the reviewer no way to open it, even though the backend serves it on a dedicated route. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/payments/page.tsx`

- **Status:** Closed — the proof is fetched with `api.download` and rendered as an object URL in the dialog, with its own loading and failed states. A plain `<a href>` cannot work: `readBearerToken` reads the `Authorization` header only.
- **Category:** `ux` · line 611
- **What a user sees:** A Super Admin approving a bank-transfer payment is told a proof image exists, is told to check the evidence, and has no control anywhere in the product that shows it. FR-BILL-004's decision is made on the transaction id alone.
- **Evidence:** payments/page.tsx:610-613 is verbatim: `<dt className="text-muted">Screenshot</dt>` / `<dd className={payment.has_screenshot ? 'text-ink' : 'text-muted-soft'}>` / `{payment.has_screenshot ? 'Attached' : 'None attached'}`. The dialog description at line 547 is verbatim `'Check the evidence against the amount before deciding.'`. Backend payments.routes.js:138-144 mounts `router.get('/:id/screenshot', requirePermission('payments.view'), ...)` verbatim, and payments.controller.js `screenshot()` calls `sendStoredFile(res, payment.screenshot_path, { filename: ..., inline: true, schoolId: payment.school_id })`. The list route is `router.get('/', requirePermission('payments.view'), ...)` — the same key. `grep -rn screenshot` over frontend/src returns only comments, the `has_screenshot` boolean and these two render sites: nothing anywhere fetches `/payments/:id/screenshot`.
- **Fix:** When `payment.has_screenshot`, render the proof in the dialog through the authenticated client — `api.download('/payments/${payment.id}/screenshot')` (exported at apiClient.ts:461, wired into `api` at :527) turned into an object URL. A plain `<a href>` genuinely will not work: middlewares/authenticate.js `readBearerToken` reads `req.get('Authorization')` only, so an unadorned navigation is a 401.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every quoted line is verbatim at the stated location, the route and its `payments.view` guard exist as described, and a grep of the whole frontend confirms no caller of the screenshot route.

### Every money card is stamped with a hardcoded USD symbol, but the figures behind them are sums over a multi-currency column with no currency filter. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 43
- **What a user sees:** A platform with one school billed in EUR and one in USD shows one summed figure with a `$` in front of it. The number is not an amount in any currency, and the glyph asserts one the payload does not carry. Same for Yearly revenue and Pending amount.
- **Evidence:** super-admin/page.tsx:38-46 is verbatim, including `style: 'currency',` / `currency: 'USD',`, and it is applied at lines 137, 142 and 146 (Monthly revenue, Yearly revenue, Pending amount). platform.service.js `revenueWhere()` is `return { ...base, status: PAYMENT_STATUS.APPROVED, [Op.or]: [ { paid_at: ... }, { paid_at: null, created_at: ... } ] };` — no `currency` key appears anywhere in the file, and `sumPayments` is a bare `db.Payment.sum('amount', { where })`. models/billing.js:261 is verbatim `currency: { type: DataTypes.STRING(10), allowNull: false, defaultValue: 'USD' },`; payments.service.js:439 is verbatim `currency: spec.currency || invoice.currency,`; plans.validation.js:247 is verbatim `currency: Joi.string().trim().uppercase().length(3),` — any ISO code. lib/money.ts:32-34 carries the opposite doctrine verbatim.
- **Fix:** Drop the local `money()` and use `formatMoney` from lib/money.ts, which prints no glyph, and label the cards so the mixed basis is visible.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The formatter is verbatim at 38-46, and I read platform.service.js end to end: `revenueWhere` and `sumPayments` contain no currency clause, so the sum genuinely spans whatever codes plans.validation.js accepts.

### The dashboard's money formatter rounds every revenue figure to whole units, silently discarding the cents the API sends. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 44
- **What a user sees:** A month whose approved payments total 1,234.56 renders as `$1,235` — above the real figure. Three cards on the revenue section report a number that cannot be reconciled against the payments list.
- **Evidence:** super-admin/page.tsx:41-45 is verbatim, including `maximumFractionDigits: 0,`. platform.service.js `sumPayments` is verbatim `const total = await db.Payment.sum('amount', { where }); return money.round(money.toNumber(total));`, and utils/money.js defines `round` as `toMajor(toMinor(value))` where `toMinor` is `Math.round(toNumber(value) * SCALE)` and `toMajor` is `Math.round(minor) / SCALE` with `SCALE = 100` — i.e. two decimal places. models/columns.js:59-61 is verbatim `const money = (options = {}) => ({ type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(14, 2), ... })`. Intl does not throw here: an explicit `maximumFractionDigits: 0` with `minimumFractionDigits` unset clamps the minimum to 0, so the call succeeds and rounds half-expand.
- **Fix:** Use `formatMoney` from lib/money.ts, pinned at `minimumFractionDigits: 2, maximumFractionDigits: 2` (money.ts:38-41).
- **Why it survived refutation:** `maximumFractionDigits: 0` is verbatim at line 44 and the service demonstrably returns a 2dp value; I also checked the Intl digit-option rules so the claim is a real rounding, not a thrown RangeError.

### Both filter controls hardcode a white background while taking their text colour from the theme token, so in dark mode they are near-white text on white. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/invoices/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 129
- **What a user sees:** A Super Admin whose OS is in dark mode — reached with no toggle, since 'system' is the default — sees the search box and status select as blank white rectangles with invisible text.
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.tsx:128-130 is verbatim: `const CONTROL_CLASS = 'rounded-md border border-border-strong bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink outline-none ' + 'focus:border-teal';`, applied at line 273 (`className={`w-full max-w-sm ${CONTROL_CLASS}`}`) and line 287 (`className={CONTROL_CLASS}`). globals.css:301 is `--color-ink: var(--ink);`; the dark block opens `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme="light"]) {` at 201-202 with `--ink: var(--slate-100);` at 203, and `:root[data-theme="dark"]` at 251 repeats it at 252; `--slate-100: #f1f3f7;` at 39. `grep bg-white app/globals.css` returns nothing, so `bg-white` is Tailwind's `#fff`. components/theme.tsx confirms 'system' is the default and `applyTheme` calls `root.removeAttribute('data-theme')` for it, so the media query governs unless the user opts out. organizations/page.tsx:211 uses `field-input` and payments/page.tsx:323 uses `field-select`, and globals.css:593-609 sets `background: var(--surface-1);` for those classes.

### The Invoice interface types the two money fields as `string`, but the driver returns DECIMAL columns as JS numbers. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/invoices/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 82
- **What a user sees:** Nothing renders wrong today, because `Number(1200.5)` is a no-op — the harm is that the declared type is false and it is the exact premise that produced the `String(value).split('.')` defect lib/money.ts was written to fix. TypeScript will not catch the next person who trusts it.
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.tsx:82-83 are verbatim `total: string;` and `amount_due: string;` inside the `interface Invoice` block at 76-87, and the local formatter at 105-110 is verbatim `function formatMoney(amount: string, currency: string): string {`. config/database.js:57-61 is verbatim `dialectOptions: { // Return DECIMAL as string-free numbers where safe; money is handled via the money util.` / `decimalNumbers: true,` / `connectTimeout: 20000,` / `},`. invoices.controller.js `present()` is `const json = invoice.toJSON(); ... return { ...json, is_overdue: ..., tax_is_inclusive: ... };` — no conversion. models/columns.js `money()` at 59-65 installs no getter. lib/money.ts:14-19 records the same correction.
- **Fix:** Type `total` and `amount_due` as `number | string`, delete the local `DECIMAL`/`formatMoney`, and use `formatCodeWithAmount(row.currency, row.total)` from lib/money.ts, which already produces `USD 1,200.00` and pins the locale to 'en-US'.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The `total: string` / `amount_due: string` lines are verbatim and `decimalNumbers: true` is verbatim in config/database.js; the finding's own text concedes no user-visible symptom, so the severity is inflated even though the claim itself holds.

### The search box and both filter selects hard-code `bg-white` with the themed `text-ink`, so in dark mode they render near-white text on a white ground. — `(platform)/super-admin/plans/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**

- **What a user sees:** In dark mode the operator sees three blank white boxes: typed search text and both selected filter values are invisible at ~1.1:1.
- **Evidence:** plans/page.tsx:80-82 verbatim: `const FILTER_CLASS =\n  'rounded-md border border-border-strong bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink outline-none ' +\n  'focus:border-teal';` applied at :262, :279, :301. globals.css:201-203 `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme="light"]) { --ink: var(--slate-100);` and :39 `--slate-100: #f1f3f7;`. The three controls are bare `<input>`/`<select>` with no `.field-input`, so nothing else sets their background; `bg-white` = #ffffff.
- **Fix:** Replace `bg-white` with `bg-surface-1`, or switch to `SearchInput` (components/table.tsx:315) and `SelectField` (components/form.tsx:126).
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote verified verbatim at plans/page.tsx:80-82 and the three application sites. Dark mode is reachable in one click, not just by OS preference: `ThemeToggle` is mounted in the app shell at components/shell.tsx:241 and :274 and components/theme.tsx:51 writes `root.setAttribute('data-theme', choice)`. globals.css declares no `@layer` of its own and defines no element-level `input`/`select` background — grep for `^input|^select|input,` in globals.css returns nothing — so the Tailwind utility is the only background rule that applies. #f1f3f7 on #ffffff is ~1.1:1.

### The file header asserts `plan_features.value` is a schemaless JSON column that may hold a boolean or a number; the model and the validator both make it a VARCHAR(120) that can only ever be a string or null. — `(platform)/super-admin/plans/features/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 21
- **What a user sees:** The comment is the contract the next author reads. It licenses sending `value: {retention: 30}` to an endpoint whose Joi string rule 422s it, and it presents `featureValue`'s boolean/number/JSON.stringify branches (features/page.tsx:43-45) as live paths when the API can never reach them.
- **Evidence:** features/page.tsx:19-23 verbatim: '`plan_features.value` is a JSON column with no schema: a feature may be a boolean toggle, a number, or a string. It is typed `unknown` and rendered through `JSON.stringify`…'. backend/src/models/subscription.js:217-218: `/** Feature value when a feature is more than a boolean (e.g. a retention window). */` / `value: { type: DataTypes.STRING(120), allowNull: true },`. The only writer, plans.validation.js:373: `value: Joi.string().trim().max(120).empty('').allow(null),` inside `featureItem`, used by `setFeatures` (:383) behind `PUT /plans/:id/features` (plans.routes.js:185).
- **Fix:** State that `value` is a nullable VARCHAR(120) constrained to a trimmed string, type `value: string | null` in components/planScope.tsx:57, and reduce `featureValue` to the null and string cases.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both quotations are verbatim; only the cited line numbers drift (the model column is at 218, not 222-223). The column is a STRING, not `json()` — the model file has a `json()` helper it uses elsewhere (`settings: json({...})` on plan_modules) and deliberately does not use here. With `Joi.string().trim().max(120).allow(null)` as the sole writer, `typeof value === 'boolean'` at features/page.tsx:43 and the `JSON.stringify(value)` fallback at :45 are unreachable through the API. The header states a schema fact that is false.

### A doc comment states that DECIMAL columns in this module arrive as strings; config/database.js sets decimalNumbers = true, so they arrive as JS numbers. — `(platform)/super-admin/plans/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 75
- **What a user sees:** No runtime symptom here, but it is the premise money.ts:1-22 says already produced two broken formatters — the next author adding a price column to this module follows the comment, types it `string`, and writes `String(value).split('.')`.
- **Evidence:** plans/page.tsx:74-76 verbatim: `/** \`INTEGER.UNSIGNED\`, so a number rather than one of the DECIMAL strings elsewhere in this module. */` / `trial_days: number;`. backend/src/config/database.js:57-59: `dialectOptions: {` / `// Return DECIMAL as string-free numbers where safe; money is handled via the money util.` / `decimalNumbers: true,`. lib/money.ts:14-19 records the same measurement. The downstream hedges are visible at components/planScope.tsx:69 `overage_unit_amount: string | number | null;` and limits/page.tsx:49.
- **Fix:** Delete the clause 'rather than one of the DECIMAL strings elsewhere in this module'; keep the accurate `INTEGER.UNSIGNED` note.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Quote verified verbatim at plans/page.tsx:75 and at database.js:57-59. There is no reading on which the clause is true: no DECIMAL anywhere in this API module arrives as a string, because `decimalNumbers: true` makes mysql2 parse it to a number before Sequelize sees it. Unlike finding 11, this is not a widened type that happens to be safe — it is a positive assertion of a false fact about the wire format, in the one file a reader of this module opens first.

### The flat renderer stringifies values that are objects, so the Expense report's whole category breakdown prints as [object Object]. — `(platform)/super-admin/reports/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 429
- **What a user sees:** The Expenses report renders two rows reading `income | by_category | [object Object]` and `expense | by_category | [object Object]`, so the salaries / other_expenses / fees / other_income figures — the only breakdown the report carries — are unreadable on screen.
- **Evidence:** FlatRow is declared `value: string | number | boolean;` (213-217) and the push casts blindly (228-229): `rows.push({ section, key, value: value as string | number | boolean });`. The walk descends exactly one level (244-247): `} else if (typeof value === 'object') { for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>)) { push(key, k, v === null ? '' : v);`. The cell prints whatever arrived (425): `<span className="tabular-nums">{String(row.value)}</span>`.
- **Fix:** Recurse in toRows for nested objects (`${k}.${nested}` keys) or special-case the two by_category maps.
- **Why it survived refutation:** finance.service.js:437-446 `function foldBuckets(rows, categories) { ... return { total, by_category: byCategory }; }` and finance.service.js:490-503 return `income`/`expense` as those two-level objects; reports.service.js:255-264 spreads them into the §22 envelope with `...report`. constants.js:480-488 confirms the four category names. So the top-level `income` object yields a `by_category` entry whose value is itself an object, and String() on it is literally "[object Object]".

### Money figures from the Fee and Expense reports are printed with raw String(), so no amount is shown to two decimals and none is paired with its currency code. — `(platform)/super-admin/reports/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 429
- **What a user sees:** An outstanding balance of 1234.50 prints as `1234.5` and a zero prints as `0` rather than `0.00`, and the currency appears only as its own `summary | currency | USD` row several rows above the amounts, on a screen that can show any school's books.
- **Evidence:** reports/page.tsx:421-426 renders every value, money included: `row.value === '' ? (<span className="text-muted-soft">—</span>) : (<span className="tabular-nums">{String(row.value)}</span>)`. The values are JS numbers: utils/money.js:25-32 `function toMajor(minor) { return Math.round(minor) / SCALE; }` / `function round(value) { return toMajor(toMinor(value)); }`, and reports.service.js:239-249 builds `billed`, `collected`, `outstanding: money.subtract(billed, collected)` from them; finance.service.js:502 `net_balance: money.subtract(income.total, expense.total),`. The import block (56-70) pulls in apiClient, auth, table and useCollection — `@/lib/money` is absent.
- **Fix:** Format the money-bearing keys with formatAmountWithCode(value, report.currency) from @/lib/money.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Not taste in this codebase: lib/money.ts:21-22 states the house rule the screen breaks — "In a money column `499` and `499.00` differ in what they claim to know, and `1,200.5` is ambiguous between five cents and fifty" — and lines 32-34 require the three-letter code beside the amount on platform screens. One caveat on the fix: `total` is also the Students/Teachers headcount and the subscription count (reports.service.js:163, 393), so a key-name-only rule would render those as "42.00"; the formatting has to be section-aware.

The same closure exists for teacher attendance at :350. ### FR-DOC-001's only real operation — generating a document — has no control anywhere, and the rendered file can never be requested. — `(school)/school/documents/page.tsx`

- **Status:** Closed — a `Generate a document` action on the Documents header posts `POST /documents` with an owner picker that follows the type (`OWNER_OF`), and every row has a PDF button calling `api.download` on `?format=pdf`. Verified live: a Teacher ID Card generated, then downloaded as 1,757 bytes beginning `%PDF-`.
- **Category:** `ux` · line 143
- **What a user sees:** An Accountant who needs a fee receipt or a Principal who needs a leaving certificate opens Documents, sees a read-only list, and has no button to generate one and no way to open the PDF the cell says is "streamed on request".
- **Evidence:** documents/page.tsx:145-148 renders `PageHeader` with no `action` prop; the file never imports `useAuth`; the only API call is line 81 `useCollection<DocumentRow>('/documents', query)`. Every row's File cell renders line 136 `<span className="text-muted-soft">streamed on request</span>`.
- **Fix:** Add a `PageHeader action` gated on `can('documents.generate')` leading to a generate form, and a per-row "Download PDF" calling `api.download` then `saveFile()` from lib/apiClient.ts.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `grep -rn "'/documents" src/` returns exactly one hit — documents/page.tsx:81 — so no screen in the app posts to the endpoint or fetches the PDF. Backend confirms both exist: documents.routes.js `router.post('/', requirePermission('documents.generate'), validate({ body: schemas.generate }), ...)` under the comment "FR-DOC-001 — the generation itself", and documents.controller.js `if (req.query.format === REPORT_FORMATS.PDF)` streaming a Buffer with a `Content-Disposition` attachment header. config/permissions.js:146 is verbatim `{ key: 'documents.generate', group: 'Documents', name: 'Generate school documents', module: null }`. The dead-cell claim holds: documents.service.js create() sets `is_generated: true` under the comment "No bytes: rendering is Phase 5.4. `file_path`, `file_name`, `mime_type` and `file_size_bytes` stay null", and `present()` returns `{ ...json, has_file: Boolean(row.file_path) }` — always false — so `fileSize()` and the `row.file_name` branch are genuinely unreachable. The download path the fix names is real and already used: reports/page.tsx:396 calls `api.download(` then `saveFile(file)`.

### `classTeacher` is declared on the row and rendered, but `GET /classes/{id}/sections` never includes the association, so the column always falls back to a raw teacher id. — `(school)/school/classes/sections/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 59
- **What a user sees:** Every section that has a class teacher shows "teacher #7" — a number a school office cannot look up.
- **Evidence:** sections/page.tsx:59 declares `classTeacher?: { id: number; first_name: string; last_name: string | null };` and 126-131 renders `row.classTeacher ? ... : row.class_teacher_id ? <span className="text-muted-soft">teacher #{row.class_teacher_id}</span> : ...`.
- **Fix:** Add `include: [{ model: db.Teacher, as: 'classTeacher', attributes: ['id', 'first_name', 'last_name'] }]` to `listSections` in classes.service.js; or delete the unreachable branch and render presence only.
- **Why it survived refutation:** classes.service.js:248-255 is verbatim, including `const rows = await db.Section.findAll({ where: tenantWhere(req.tenant, { class_id: klass.id }), order: [['name', 'ASC']], });` — no `include`. classes.controller.js `listSections` is `const { rows } = await service.listSections(req, req.params.id); return ApiResponse.ok(res, { sections: rows });` — no present(), no shaping. models/index.js has `Section.belongsTo(Teacher, { as: 'classTeacher', foreignKey: 'class_teacher_id' });` two lines below the Class one, so the association exists and is simply never loaded. The `row.classTeacher` branch is therefore dead on every row and every section with a teacher renders `teacher #N`. The fix adds no table, column, permission or limit key, so it is SRS-safe.

### The search box promises to search descriptions; the service only LIKEs the title. — `(school)/school/documents/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `ux` · line 156
- **What a user sees:** A receptionist who remembers a phrase from a document's description types it, gets "No document matches these filters.", and concludes the document was never generated.

The contrast case is real too: teachers.service.js:203-209 is verbatim `if (query.q) { where[Op.or] = [{ first_name: ... }, { last_name: ... }, { employee_id: ... }]; }`. The empty-state text the impact depends on is real — documents/page.tsx:188 `{search || documentType ? 'No document matches these filters.' : 'No documents yet.'}`. `description` is an existing column (documents.service.js create() writes `description: payload.description || null`), so widening the search invents nothing.

The @theme block at globals.css:300 is `@theme inline`, which does not clear Tailwind's default palette, so `bg-white` is still literally #fff, and :301 `--color-ink: var(--ink)` is what `text-ink` compiles through. The suggested `field-select` exists at globals.css:657 with its own dark chevron at :665-667.

### The Fees screen is a read-only queue: neither fee assignment nor payment collection has any control anywhere in the frontend, though the header comment claims assignment is "an action". — `(school)/school/fees/page.tsx`

- **Status:** Closed — `Assign` on every active structure row posts `POST /fees/assignments` with a `MultiSelectField` of active students, and `Record payment` on every ledger row with a balance posts `POST /fees/payments`. Verified live: 950.00 charged, 300.00 collected, ledger read `650.00 pending / partially paid`, receipt `RCP-202609-00001` issued.
- **Category:** `ux` · line 12
- **What a user sees:** A school can define what it charges and never charge anyone: the ledger has no populating path from the UI, so its empty notice describes a step the product does not offer, and fees.collect is a permission nothing can exercise.
- **Evidence:** fees/page.tsx:12-13 is verbatim ("`/fees/assignments` is the POST that creates these and has no GET, which is why assignment is an action rather than a fourth tab"). `grep -rn "fees/assignments|fees/payments" frontend/src` returns exactly three hits: the two header comment lines (:12, :14) and the GET at :262 `useCollection<PaymentRow>('/fees/payments', query)`. The only control on the screen is the structures link at :152. Both endpoints exist: fees.routes.js:108-114 `router.post('/assignments', requirePermission('fees.manage'), …)` and :125-131 `router.post('/payments', requirePermission('fees.collect'), …)`. Grepping the backend for writers of the ledger finds only fees.service.js:393 `db.StudentFee.bulkCreate(rows, …)` inside assign() and :555 `db.FeePayment.create(` inside pay().
- **Fix:** Add an "Assign to students" action on Structures rows posting /fees/assignments and a "Record payment" action on Ledger rows posting /fees/payments (gated on fees.collect).
- **Why it survived refutation:** The grep result is exactly as claimed, and StudentFee rows have only one creator: `db.StudentFee.bulkCreate(rows, { validate: true, transaction });` at fees.service.js:393, reachable only through POST /fees/assignments — which nothing in frontend/src calls. The fix uses existing endpoints and permissions, so it does not touch the fixed catalogue.

### /finance/report is called with no currency, so a school holding two currencies gets a permanent error where the Net Balance should be, with a retry that can never succeed. — `(school)/school/finance/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `money` · line 265
- **What a user sees:** A school that ever recorded an entry in a second currency sees a red panel at the top of Finance permanently, with a dead retry, and the screen discards the detail in which the server named the currencies.
- **Evidence:** finance/page.tsx:265-267 is verbatim `const result = await api.get<{ report: FinanceReport }>('/finance/report', {` / `signal: controller.signal,` / `});` — no query. finance.service.js:481-488 is verbatim, including `if (!currency && currencies.length > 1) {` and `throw ApiError.validation(`. ApiError.js:66-68 makes that 422 VALIDATION_ERROR, absent from EXPLAINED_CODES, so finance/page.tsx:275 `setError(caught.message)` and :285 `<ErrorNotice message={error} onRetry={() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1)} />` render, and the retry re-sends the identical request. The parameter exists: finance.validation.js:243-247 declares `const report = Joi.object({` … `currency: shared.currency,` … and shared.currency is `Joi.string().trim().uppercase().max(10),` at :55.
- **Fix:** Add a currency selector to NetBalance and pass currency; on the 422 read caught.fieldErrors().currency and offer the named currencies instead of a dead retry.

Caveat on reachability: with no income/expense creation screen (the dead-link finding), a mixed-currency ledger can only arrive through the API or a seed today; the finding presents the report block as a paraphrased one-liner rather than the four real lines, though the range 243-247 is correct.

### The Status column renders the stored `status`, which never holds `overdue`, so the "Overdue" filter returns rows every one of which is badged "Issued", and the `is_overdue` field the API sends is discarded. — `(school)/school/library/page.tsx`

- **Fix:** Declare `is_overdue: boolean; days_overdue: number;` on LoanRow and render `<StatusBadge status={row.is_overdue ? 'overdue' : row.status} />`, with `days_overdue` beside the Due date.
