### `unplaced` is a single string, so when both model-level validators fail the user is shown only one of the two errors and has to submit twice to discover the second. — `(school)/school/fees/structures/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 328
- **What a user sees:** Choose a fine type and a discount type, leave both amounts blank, and the banner reports only the second problem; fixing it reveals the first — two round trips for one form.
- **Evidence:** fees/structures/new/page.tsx:321-329 is verbatim, including `let unplaced: string | null = null;` and `          else unplaced = message;`. Both validators exist and are independent — models/finance.js:86 `fineTypeNeedsAmount() {` and :91 `discountTypeNeedsAmount() {` — and fees.validation.js declares fine_amount/fine_type and discount_amount/discount_type as independent optional fields (:92-96), so Joi does not catch the pairing first. createStructure routes the failure through rethrow (fees.service.js:211), whose ValidationError branch at :133-134 maps `err.errors` one-to-one: `throw ApiError.validation(err.message, err.errors.map((e) => ({ field: e.path, message: e.message })));`. apiClient.ts fieldErrors() keys by field name and skips only empty fields, so both survive; formErrors() returns nothing for them, and the else branch overwrites.
- **Fix:** Make unplaced a string[], push into it, and join with formErrors() the way homework/new/page.tsx:352-360 does.
- **Why it survived refutation:** fees.service.js:133-134 really is the one-to-one mapping quoted, Sequelize sets a model-level validator's ValidationErrorItem.path to the validator name (so neither key is in FORM_FIELDS), and homework/new/page.tsx:354-357 does the right thing: `const unplaced = caught.formErrors();` … `unplaced.push(message);`. Citation drift of one line on the service quote (133-134, cited 134-136).

### Eight selects and a textarea are hand-rolled instead of using SelectField/TextAreaField, so none has aria-invalid or aria-describedby, the required Component field never says it is required, and the selects are styled as text boxes. — `(school)/school/fees/structures/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 391
- **What a user sees:** On a 422 the rejection is not announced and the red text is not associated with the control; the third mandatory field carries no visible "required" while Name and Amount do.
- **Evidence:** fees/structures/new/page.tsx:387-397 is verbatim (label, then `<select id="component" required … className={INPUT_CLASS}>`), followed at :405-408 by `{fieldErrors.component ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">…` — the <p> carries no id, so no aria-describedby target exists. The same shape recurs at the selects on :444, :496, :539, :579, :634, :676 and the textarea on :700 (grep of `<select|<textarea` confirms those exact lines). form.tsx:126-156 shows SelectField wiring `required={required}`, `aria-invalid={Boolean(error)}` and `aria-describedby={error ? errorId : hint ? hintId : undefined}` plus a FieldLabel that prints the word "required" (:46-49); form.tsx:14-17 states the bypassed contract. INPUT_CLASS (:145-147) applies field-input, not the .field-select defined at globals.css:657-663.
- **Fix:** Replace each hand-rolled block with SelectField / TextAreaField, passing required, error and hint, and delete INPUT_CLASS.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every cited line number is exact, and the aria gap is real: form.tsx:150-151 wires `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` while nothing on these selects does. Two overstatements to discount: there are seven selects plus a textarea, not eight and nine; and `required` on a <select> maps to aria-required in the accessibility tree, so a screen reader IS told the field is required — the missing "required" is visual only.

### Six selects and the description textarea bypass SelectField/TextAreaField, so the required Class field never says it is required and no select is wired for aria-invalid/aria-describedby. — `(school)/school/homework/new/page.tsx`

The hint/error <p> at :452-462 carries no id. The two Field-rendered required inputs at :414-423 and :629 do get the wiring via form.tsx:99-111. The form is `noValidate` at :413. INPUT_CLASS at :131-133 is field-input, not .field-select (globals.css:657-663).
- **Fix:** Use SelectField and TextAreaField for all seven controls, passing required, error and hint; drop INPUT_CLASS.

Same correction as the sibling finding: `required` on a select still exposes aria-required to assistive tech, so "only two announce themselves as required" is wrong — the deficit is the visible label and the unassociated error text.

### The required class picker is capped at one page of 100 with no search, so homework cannot be set for any class beyond the hundredth. — `(school)/school/homework/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)

- **What a user sees:** At a school with more than 100 classes, classes ranked 101+ can never be given homework through the UI, and the form says so without offering a way through.

The cap is real: constants.js:751-755 sets `MAX_LIMIT: 100` and validate.js:145-150 pins `limit` to `.max(PAGINATION.MAX_LIMIT)`. classes.service.js:85 orders `['numeric_order', 'ASC']`. class_id is required at homework.validation.js:70 `  class_id: fields.class_id.required(),`. The form's own shortfall message is at :458-462: "The first {classes.rows.length} of {classes.total} classes. A page cannot hold more." No text input, second page or type-ahead exists.

### Two of the three search boxes fire a request per keystroke with no debounce, and each in-flight response unmounts the table. — `(school)/school/fees/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 148
- **What a user sees:** Typing "transport" issues nine requests and makes the table disappear and reappear nine times, losing scroll position each time.
- **Evidence:** fees/page.tsx:147-148 is verbatim `          value={search}` / `          onChange={(event) => { setSearch(event.target.value); setPage(1); }}`, feeding :115 `const query = useMemo(() => ({ page, limit: 20, q: search || undefined }), [page, search]);` and :116's useCollection; Payments repeats it at :285. useCollection.ts:147-152 sets `setLoading(true)` on every query change (the key is JSON.stringify of the query, :132-137), and fees/page.tsx:160 then renders `: loading ? <p className="text-sm text-muted">Loading…</p>` in place of the table. No setTimeout exists in the file. table.tsx:12-14 records the intent — "`DataTable` now dims in place while `busy`" — and `busy` (table.tsx:161) is never passed at :164, :247 or :296.
- **Fix:** Debounce 300ms as exams/page.tsx:158-168 does and pass busy={loading} to DataTable instead of swapping the table for a <p>.
- **Why it survived refutation:** useCollection.ts:150 is `setLoading(true);` inside the effect keyed on stableQuery, and DataTable's signature at table.tsx:164 is `({ columns, rows, rowKey, caption, busy = false })` — the three call sites in fees/page.tsx pass no busy. exams/page.tsx:158-168 does debounce with `setTimeout(…, 300)`, so the inconsistency is real.

### The password-mismatch check is the one rejection path that writes no banner and does not move focus, so submitting a long form can look like nothing happened. — `(school)/school/parents/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**

Every other failure reaches the banner region at :385-390 — `setRefusal(...)` at :322, `setNotices(banner)` at :350, `setNotices([caught.message])` at :351 and :353. This branch returns before `setSaving(true)` (:264) and there is no ref or focus call on the confirmation Field (:444-453).

The distance is real: the Confirm password Field is at 444-453 and `<SubmitButton>` at 697-700, with the contact block, the child picker (549-683) and the Audit field between them.

### Five hand-rolled controls bypass the SelectField/Field primitives, so their error and helper text is wired to nothing — no aria-invalid, no aria-describedby, and no id on the message. — `(school)/school/parents/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 459
- **What a user sees:** A screen-reader user whose `is_active` or child relation the server rejects tabs back through the form and hears the label and value with no indication that the field is in error or what the message says.
- **Evidence:** parents/new/page.tsx:459-470 `<select id="is_active" value={values.is_active} onChange={set('is_active')} className={INPUT_CLASS}> … {fieldErrors.is_active ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">{fieldErrors.is_active}</p>`; same shape at 553-563 (`child-search`, whose "Showing N of M matches" paragraph at 564-568 has no id), 576-595 (`child-pick`) and 645-654 (`child-relation-${child.student.id}`), whose per-row error at 673-677 is likewise unlinked. The primitives are imported in the same file (:85 `import { Field, Notice, SubmitButton } from '@/components/form';`) and components/form.tsx:14-15 states the guarantee, implemented by Field (:93-114) and SelectField (:126-160) as `aria-invalid={Boolean(error)} aria-describedby={error ? errorId : hint ? hintId : undefined}`.
- **Fix:** Use SelectField for `is_active` and `child-pick`, Field for `child-search` and each `child-relation-*`, passing `error={…}` and moving the "Showing N of M matches" copy into `hint`; that also deletes INPUT_CLASS (:91-93).
- **Why it survived refutation:** All five controls are verbatim at the cited lines, each styled with the local `INPUT_CLASS` (91-93) and each followed by a bare `<p>` with no id. form.tsx:14-15 reads "`aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` wired to whichever of hint/error is showing, so the message reaches a screen reader at the field rather than as a banner somewhere else", and Field (93-114) and SelectField (126-160) both contain the quoted attribute pair. The import at :85 is exact, so the primitives are already in scope.

### The Description textarea is hand-rolled instead of using TextAreaField, so its error and hint text is not associated with the control. — `(school)/school/library/books/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 339
- **What a user sees:** When the server rejects the description (library.validation.js:56 bounds it at 5000 characters), a screen-reader user hears no error on the field — the message is rendered but unreachable from the control.
- **Evidence:** books/new/page.tsx:339-354 `<textarea id="description" rows={3} maxLength={5000} value={values.description} onChange={set('description')} className={INPUT_CLASS} /> {fieldErrors.description ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">{fieldErrors.description}</p>) : (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-muted">` — no aria-invalid, no aria-describedby, no id on either paragraph. `description` is in CONTROLLED (:112) precisely so a 422 can land here. components/form.tsx:171-200 exports TextAreaField, which wires both.
- **Fix:** `<TextAreaField id="description" label="Description" rows={3} maxLength={5000} value={values.description} onChange={set('description')} error={fieldErrors.description} hint="…" />`, which also removes the local INPUT_CLASS (:82-84).

TextAreaField begins at form.tsx:171 and contains `aria-invalid={Boolean(error)}` / `aria-describedby={error ? errorId : hint ? hintId : undefined}`. library.validation.js:56 is exactly `  description: Joi.string().trim().max(5000).empty('').allow(null),`. Only `description`'s position in CONTROLLED is off by one — it is line 112, not 113.

The two sibling debounces are verbatim at homework:152-162 and parents/new:185-188. library.service.js:204-208 is the three-way `[Op.or]` on title/author/isbn with `%${query.q}%` on each.

### Every select and textarea on the form is hand-rolled with `INPUT_CLASS` instead of the shared `SelectField`/`TextAreaField`, so none of them gets `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby` or the red error border. — `(school)/school/staff/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 392
- **What a user sees:** When the server rejects one of these controls, a screen-reader user hears no error at the field — the message is an unassociated paragraph — and no control is marked invalid. Sighted users get a red sentence under a select that still has a normal grey border, unlike every `Field` on the same form.
- **Evidence:** staff/new/page.tsx:392-419 `<label htmlFor="category" className="block text-sm font-medium">Category</label><select id="category" required value={values.category} onChange={set('category')} className={INPUT_CLASS}>` … `{fieldErrors.category ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">{fieldErrors.category}</p>) : ...}`. Same shape for `is_active` (:470-493), `gender` (:528-545), `user_id` (:611-625), `notes` (:646-653) and `metadata` (:665-672). components/form.tsx:126-159 `SelectField` and :171-199 `TextAreaField` wire `aria-invalid={Boolean(error)}` and `aria-describedby={error ? errorId : hint ? hintId : undefined}`, and globals.css:644-648 keys the red border off `[aria-invalid="true"]`.
- **Fix:** Replace each hand-rolled block with `SelectField` / `TextAreaField` from components/form.tsx, passing `error={fieldErrors.x}`, `hint=` and `required`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every quoted block exists verbatim at the stated lines, and form.tsx:150-151 / :192-193 do wire `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` while the hand-rolled blocks wire neither — so the error paragraphs are genuinely unassociated and globals.css:644-646 `.field-select[aria-invalid="true"] { border-color: var(--danger); }` can never fire. One sub-claim in the finding is WRONG and should not be repeated: `noValidate` at :380 does not make `required` at :398 "inert" for assistive technology — the HTML `required` attribute maps to the accessibility required state regardless of constraint validation, so a screen reader does announce Category as required. What is genuinely missing there is only the visible "required" text that components/form.tsx:46-50 renders. The load-bearing defect (unassociated errors, no invalid state) is real.

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 733
- **What a user sees:** A blind user who mistypes the metadata JSON or picks a section the server rejects gets a red paragraph that is never announced with the field, and no control is marked invalid.
- **Evidence:** students/new/page.tsx:733-749 `<label htmlFor="gender" className="block text-sm font-medium">Gender</label><select id="gender" value={values.gender} onChange={set('gender')} className={INPUT_CLASS}>` … `{fieldErrors.gender ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">{fieldErrors.gender}</p>) : null}`; identically for `admission_session_id` (:564-581), `class_id`, `section_id` (:660-680), `academic_session_id` (:695-709), `uses_transport` (:932-943), and the `notes` (:965-972) and `metadata` (:984-991) textareas. components/form.tsx:126-159 and :171-199 already wire `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby`; globals.css:644-648 drives the error border from `[aria-invalid="true"]`. The client-side JSON error at :425-427 is one of the messages that lands on an unmarked control.
- **Fix:** Swap each block for `SelectField` / `TextAreaField`, passing `error` and `hint`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Every quoted block is verbatim at the stated lines, and none of the seven controls sets `aria-invalid` or `aria-describedby`. Worth recording for whoever fixes it: `INPUT_CLASS` at :151-153 already ends in `'field-input '`, so these controls do get the themed background — the defect is purely the missing programmatic association and invalid state, not the styling. The metadata message at :425-427 `metadata: 'This is not valid JSON. A JSON object looks like {"house": "Blue"}.'` is set client-side and lands on the unmarked textarea at :984-991, so the failure needs no server round trip to reach.

### When the session fetch fails, the "Current session" select is disabled and empty but keeps the hint that describes a working control — only the "Admission session" select above it explains the failure. — `(school)/school/students/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 710
- **What a user sees:** A receptionist without `sessions.view` sees a greyed-out "Current session" dropdown showing "Not tied to a session", under a paragraph confidently explaining how to use it. The first select says why it is dead; the second looks broken.
- **Evidence:** Both selects share `disabled={loadingOptions || sessions.failed}` (:568 and :699), fed by one `Promise.allSettled` at :302-309. The admission-session block handles the failure at :586-591 `) : sessions.failed ? (<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-muted">The session list could not be loaded, so neither session can be chosen here...` — the current-session chain at :710-720 goes straight from `fieldErrors.academic_session_id` to the descriptive hint with no `sessions.failed` branch. `GET /sessions` needs a separate grant: sessions.routes.js:31-33 `requirePermission('sessions.view')`.
- **Fix:** Add the same `: sessions.failed ? (...)` branch to the current-session message chain.
- **Why it survived refutation:** I read :560-600 and :691-721: the admission block has the three-way chain ending in the `sessions.failed` explanation at :586-591; the academic block's chain at :710-720 has only two branches. Both are disabled by the same expression (`disabled={loadingOptions || sessions.failed}` at :568 and :699), fed by the one `Promise.allSettled` at :302-305 through `settle()` at :209-213, which sets `failed: true` on any rejection. The scenario is the shipped default, not hypothetical: config/permissions.js:344-359 lists ROLES.RECEPTIONIST with `'students.manage'` but no `'sessions.view'`, while sessions.routes.js:33 is `requirePermission('sessions.view')`.

### The table is unmounted and replaced by a bare `<p>Loading…</p>` on every debounced keystroke, filter change and page turn — the regression the shared DataTable's `busy` prop and `LoadingBlock` were added to fix. — `(school)/school/parents/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 272
- **What a user sees:** Typing in the search box collapses the whole table to one line of text after each 300 ms pause, the page height jumps to nothing and back, and scroll position in a 20-row list is lost on every refinement.
- **Evidence:** parents/page.tsx:271-272 `) : loading ? (<p className="text-sm text-muted">Loading…</p>`, and `DataTable` at :290 is never passed `busy`. lib/useCollection.ts:147-150 `useEffect(() => { const controller = new AbortController(); setLoading(true);` with dep array `[path, stableQuery, nonce]` at :183; `stableQuery` is `useMemo(() => JSON.parse(key), [key])` at :137 over the serialised query, which changes whenever `debounced`, `active` or `page` changes (parents/page.tsx:144-147). components/table.tsx:12-15 states the contract: "`DataTable` now dims in place while `busy`, and the skeleton is only for the first load."
- **Fix:** Render `<LoadingBlock />` only when `loading && rows.length === 0`, and otherwise keep the table mounted with `<DataTable ... busy={loading} />`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Verbatim at :271-272 and :290. I read useCollection.ts:132-183: `setLoading(true)` at :150 is inside the effect whose deps are `[path, stableQuery, nonce]` (:183), and `stableQuery` compares by value via the `JSON.stringify` key at :132-137 — so it changes on every page turn, filter change and debounced keystroke, not just the first load. The intended pattern exists and is used: `(platform)/super-admin/payments/page.tsx:413-419` passes `busy={loading}`, and `LoadingBlock` is exported at components/table.tsx:132 but never imported by this file.

### Same regression: the table is replaced by a bare `<p>Loading…</p>` on every debounced keystroke and filter change instead of dimming in place. — `(school)/school/staff/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 363
- **What a user sees:** Every 300 ms pause while typing a name blanks the staff table, and each category or status change does the same; the page collapses and re-expands, losing scroll position on a long list.
- **Evidence:** staff/page.tsx:362-363 `) : loading ? (<p className="text-sm text-muted">Loading…</p>`, with `<DataTable columns={columns} rows={rows} rowKey={(row) => row.id} caption="Staff" />` at :377 taking no `busy`. lib/useCollection.ts:150 calls `setLoading(true)` inside the effect keyed on `[path, stableQuery, nonce]` (:183), and the query here is rebuilt from `[page, debounced, category, active]` (staff/page.tsx:156-165). components/table.tsx:12-15 documents the intended behaviour, and `LoadingBlock` (components/table.tsx:132) is not imported.
- **Fix:** Use `LoadingBlock` for the first load only and pass `busy={loading}` to `DataTable` thereafter.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Verbatim at :362-363 and :377, with the query memo at :156-165 depending on `[page, debounced, category, active]`. This screen has three filters plus a search box, so it triggers the unmount more often than the parents screen. `grep -rln LoadingBlock app/` returns only super-admin/page.tsx, super-admin/payments/page.tsx and teacher/page.tsx — this file is not among them, and no `busy=` is passed to any DataTable in it.

### The results table is unmounted and replaced by a bare `<p>Loading…</p>` on every page turn and filter change rather than dimming in place. — `(school)/school/results/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 380
- **What a user sees:** Paging through results collapses the table to one line and back on every click, so the reader loses their place in a rank-ordered list where position on the page is the whole point.
- **Evidence:** results/page.tsx:379-380 `) : loading ? (<p className="text-sm text-muted">Loading…</p>`, and `<DataTable columns={columns} rows={rows} rowKey={(row) => row.id} caption="Exam results" />` at :396 is given no `busy`. The query at :202-205 is keyed on `[page, published]`, and lib/useCollection.ts:150 sets `loading` true on every dependency change. `LoadingBlock` (components/table.tsx:132) is unused here.
- **Fix:** `LoadingBlock` on first load, `busy={loading}` on the `DataTable` afterwards.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Verbatim at :379-380 and :396; the query memo at :202-205 is `[page, published]`. The finding is correctly narrower here than for the other two screens — this page has no search box, so only page turns and the publication filter trigger it, and both do, because `stableQuery` (useCollection.ts:132-137) compares by serialised value.

### A 422 whose details all bind to inputs sets the top-level banner to null, so an 18-field form reports failure only far above the submit button, with no scroll or focus. — `(school)/school/staff/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — found already fixed when re-checked against the tree; the status line had not been updated

The submit button is at :696 and the four required fields are `employee_id` (:381), `category` (:396), `first_name` (:422) and `joining_date` (:459). No `scrollIntoView`, `focus()` or `useRef` appears anywhere in the file.

### The parent directory has no row action at all, so the sub-resource §15.2 and the permission name are built around — a parent's children — is reachable only at creation time and never afterwards. — `(school)/school/parents/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — found already fixed when re-checked against the tree; the status line had not been updated
- **Category:** `ux` · line 40
- **What a user sees:** Once a parent is created, a child can never be added to or removed from their account. A parent whose second child enrols cannot be linked, and a mis-linked child cannot be unlinked — the parent portal then shows the wrong family permanently.
- **Evidence:** The file header at :42-46 acknowledges the gap: "§15.2's headline feature is *Multiple Children*, so the column this list most wants is 'how many children, and which'... The children live at `GET /parents/:id/children`, a sub-resource". The routes exist — parents.routes.js:77-82 (children list, `requirePermission('parents.view')`), :84-90 `POST '/:id/children'` with `requirePermission('parents.manage')` (:86), :92-98 `DELETE '/:id/children/:linkId'` with `requirePermission('parents.manage')` (:94) — and parents.controller.js:43-45 answers with `ApiResponse.ok(res, { children: rows })`. config/permissions.js:91 is `{ key: 'parents.manage', group: 'People', name: 'Create parents & link children', module: MODULES.PARENT_PORTAL }`. The columns array ends at the Status badge (:181-197), there is no `parents/[id]` route, and no request in the app targets `/parents/:id/children`.
- **Fix:** Add a parent detail route (or a modal from the row) reading `GET /parents/:id/children` with link/unlink against `POST`/`DELETE /parents/:id/children`, gated on `can('parents.manage')` with a ConfirmDialog on unlink.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The header quote is verbatim at :42-46 and config/permissions.js:91 is exact to the character. The routes are real, though one citation is misplaced: the children list is at parents.routes.js:77-82, not :100-102 (which is `GET '/:id'`, coincidentally also `requirePermission('parents.view')`); the POST at :84-86 and DELETE at :94 are exactly as cited. The "creation time only" premise checks out — parents.validation.js:116-124 gives `create` a `children: Joi.array().items(...).max(50)` and parents/new/page.tsx:299-317 posts it — and `find app -name page.tsx` shows no `parents/[id]`, while frontend/src contains no `api.del` at all, so `DELETE /parents/:id/children/:linkId` is unreachable.

### A successful create navigates away with no confirmation; `useToast` exists, is mounted app-wide, and its own docblock names this exact gap as the reason it was written. — `frontend/src/app/(school)/school/teachers/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 329
- **What a user sees:** No confirmation that the teacher was created, and the list cannot show it: teachers.service.js:213 orders by `getSort({ query }, SORTABLE, ['first_name', 'ASC'])`, so the new row lands alphabetically, possibly pages away. The usual reaction is to resubmit, which then fails with TEACHER_EMPLOYEE_ID_TAKEN and reads as if the first attempt broke.
- **Evidence:** teachers/new/page.tsx:326-329 is verbatim: `      await api.post('/teachers', body);` / `      /* \`replace\`, not \`push\` — the exemplar's reason: Back would re-open a form already answered. */` / `      router.replace('/school/teachers');`. subjects/new/page.tsx:143-146 is the same shape. Neither file imports `@/components/toast` (its imports are lines 91-100: next/navigation, react, apiClient, auth, form, table, useCollection). components/toast.tsx:6-9 is verbatim, including "a create screen navigated away on success and said nothing". app/layout.tsx:5 `import { ToastProvider } from '@/components/toast';` and line 74 mounts it; `grep -rl useToast` finds only super-admin/payments/page.tsx.
- **Fix:** `const { toast } = useToast()` in both create screens and fire a success toast before `router.replace(...)`, mirroring super-admin/payments/page.tsx.
- **Why it survived refutation:** All four quotes verified verbatim, including the toast docblock. I additionally checked the impact claim rather than taking it: teachers.service.js:213's default sort is `['first_name', 'ASC']` and teachers.validation.js's list schema declares no sortBy default of its own — so "no default sort by recency" is true, and the new row really is not visible on return.

### The linked-account picker has no loading state, so on first paint and on every search it renders as an empty dropdown — the exact failure its own comment says it was built to avoid. — `frontend/src/app/(school)/school/teachers/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 235
- **What a user sees:** While `GET /users?limit=…&sortBy=name` is in flight — on mount and after each 300 ms debounce at line 209 — the picker reads as "this school has no accounts", so the operator submits without the link that FR-TEACHER-002 resolves the teacher dashboard from.
- **Evidence:** teachers/new/page.tsx:191-193 is verbatim `  const [users, setUsers] = useState<UserOption[]>([]);` / `  const [userTotal, setUserTotal] = useState(0);` / `  const [usersFailed, setUsersFailed] = useState(false);` — no in-flight flag among the six pieces of picker state (191-196). The effect at 235-268 sets `setUsers(page.data);` only on resolve and `setUsersFailed(true)` only on reject, with deps `}, [can, userQuery]);`. The render branches on `usersFailed` alone (604), so while in flight it falls to the else and draws `                <option value="">No linked account</option>` at 631 with nothing after it. Lines 232-233 name the requirement: "so a failure is reported as 'the list is unavailable' rather than left as an empty dropdown that reads 'there are no accounts'".
- **Fix:** Add a `usersLoading` flag set true before the fetch and cleared in a finally, rendering a disabled "Loading accounts…" select while true, on each `userQuery` change as well as on mount.

(` at 604, so there is no third branch for the pending case. Corroborating detail the finding did not use — the failure copy at 606-609 tells the user "the link added later from the record itself", a record screen that does not exist.

### The Timetable shortcut is captioned "Your teaching periods" but lands on the whole-school register, which offers no teacher filter control at all. — `(teacher)/teacher/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)

- **What a user sees:** A teacher clicks a card promising their own periods and gets page 1 of a 20-row register of the whole school ordered by weekday, with no control that can narrow it to them and a search box that only matches period labels and rooms.

The destination's header at :311 says "Every scheduled period in the school", and its query object (lines 157-170) sends only `page`, `limit: 20`, `q`, `day_of_week` and `is_active` — the file's own comment at :143-146 explains the six id filters are unwired. `timetable.service.js:338-343` is verbatim `if (query.q) { where[Op.or] = [ { period_label: { [Op.like]: ... } }, { room: { [Op.like]: ... } }, ]; }`, so the search cannot match a teacher name. `GET /timetable/teacher/:teacherId` is mounted at `timetable.routes.js:88-93` and no screen calls it.

### Five NOT NULL columns are typed nullable and three DECIMALs are typed as possibly `string`, contradicting `decimalNumbers: true` and leaving a dead null branch in the table. — `(student)/student/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 54
- **What a user sees:** The `row.subjects_failed === null` branch at line 131 is unreachable dead code, and the phantom `string` half of the union is what justifies the `toFixed` rounding above it — two screens over one table now disagree about the type of the same column.
- **Evidence:** Lines 54-60 are verbatim (`total_full_marks: string | number | null;` through `subjects_failed: number | null;`) and line 64 is verbatim `/** DECIMAL columns arrive as strings; parsed for display only. */`. `config/database.js:57-59` is verbatim `dialectOptions: { // Return DECIMAL as string-free numbers where safe; money is handled via the money util.` / `decimalNumbers: true,`. `models/exams.js:297-305` shows all five as NOT NULL with defaults: `total_full_marks: { type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(9, 2), allowNull: false, defaultValue: 0 }`, `total_marks_obtained` likewise, `percentage: { type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(6, 3), allowNull: false, defaultValue: 0 }`, `subjects_count` and `subjects_failed` as `INTEGER.UNSIGNED, allowNull: false, defaultValue: 0`. `exams.controller.js:213` sends the rows unmapped (`ApiResponse.paginated(res, result, pagination)`), so nothing can introduce a null. The school screen reading the same rows declares `total_full_marks: number;` at `(school)/school/results/page.tsx:123` and states the reason at :116.
- **Fix:** Type the three DECIMALs and both counters as `number`, matching `(school)/school/results/page.tsx:121-133`; delete the "arrive as strings" comment and the null branch at line 131.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `decimalNumbers: true` sits at database.js:59 exactly as quoted, the five columns are all `allowNull: false, defaultValue: 0` at models/exams.js:297-305, and the controller does no mapping — so the nullable/string unions describe values the API cannot send.

### `TEACHER_PROFILE_MISSING` — a permanent structural condition — is rendered as a generic "Something went wrong" with a Try again button that can never succeed. — `(teacher)/teacher/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 58
- **What a user sees:** An account holding `teachers.dashboard.view` with no linked `teachers` row sees a red fault banner reading "Something went wrong" and a Try again button that will fail every time. The real remedy — the school office must link the account — is never stated.
- **Evidence:** Lines 56-61 are verbatim, including `} else if (caught instanceof ApiError) { setError(caught.message);`, and line 88 is `<ErrorNotice message={error} onRetry={() => setNonce((n) => n + 1)} />`. `components/table.tsx:90-95` is verbatim: `<p className="mt-4 text-sm font-semibold text-ink">Something went wrong</p>` … `<button type="button" onClick={onRetry} className="btn btn-secondary mt-5">` … `Try again`. Backend `teachers.service.js:384-389` is verbatim `throw ApiError.notFound('No teacher profile is linked to this account', { code: 'TEACHER_PROFILE_MISSING', details: { user_id: req.user.id } });`. `lib/useCollection.ts:75-100` lists `EXPLAINED_CODES` and the code is absent (the set ends at `'STUDENT_NOT_LINKED'`), and that file's comment at :67-70 records the same failure shape being fixed once already. The state is reachable: `models/people.js:9` says a teacher's `user_id` "is nullable only where a school records someone who has no portal login yet", so a Teacher-role account can exist with no linked row, and `(teacher)/teacher/layout.tsx` gates on nothing but the shell.
- **Fix:** Branch on `TEACHER_PROFILE_MISSING` before the generic `setError` and render `RefusalNotice`, adding an `EXPLANATIONS` entry in `components/table.tsx:47-65` beside `STUDENT_NOT_LINKED`.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The code is thrown verbatim at teachers.service.js:386 and is genuinely not in the `EXPLAINED_CODES` set (useCollection.ts:75-100, which ends `'STUDENT_NOT_LINKED',`), so the `else if (caught instanceof ApiError)` arm at line 58 catches it and ErrorNotice's hard-coded heading and retry button at table.tsx:90-95 are what the user gets.

### Eight hand-rolled selects bypass the shared `SelectField`, so none carries `aria-invalid` or `aria-describedby` and their error paragraphs have no ids. — `(school)/school/timetable/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 452
- **What a user sees:** A screen-reader user submitting the form hears nothing when a select is rejected — the 422 text is painted in red with no programmatic association to the control. The two required selects also carry no visible "required" marker, unlike the three `Field` inputs beside them.
- **Evidence:** Every cited location is exact: `<select id="class_id" required ... className={INPUT_CLASS}>` at 452-458, and the same block shape for `section_id` (507), `day_of_week` (555, also `required` at 557), `subject_id` (638), `teacher_id` (693), `academic_session_id` (743), `is_break` (807) and `is_active` (833). Their error paragraphs are plain `<p className="mt-1 text-sm text-danger">{fieldErrors.x}</p>` with no `id` (e.g. 476-478, 843-844). `grep -n "aria-invalid\|aria-describedby"` over the whole file returns nothing. `components/form.tsx` provides `SelectField` (function at :119) which sets `required={required}`, `aria-invalid={Boolean(error)}`, `aria-describedby={error ? errorId : hint ? hintId : undefined}` and `className={`field-select ...`}`, and its header at :14-17 is verbatim the guarantee quoted, including "**A required field says so in words, not with a red asterisk alone.**" `FieldLabel` renders the literal word "required" at :44-48, which is why `period_number` (574-578), `start_time` (599-603) and `end_time` (608-612) are marked and the two required selects are not; the form is `noValidate` at :434.
- **Fix:** Replace the eight `<label>`+`<select>`+`<p>` blocks with `SelectField` from `@/components/form`, passing `error={fieldErrors.x}`, `hint` and `required` on Class and Day.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Grep confirms all eight selects at the exact claimed lines and zero occurrences of `aria-invalid` or `aria-describedby` in the file, against `form.tsx`'s SelectField which wires both. One sub-claim overreaches: the page description at :428 does say in words "A class, a day, a period number and the two clock times are required", so sighted users are not entirely without notice — the a11y association defect stands regardless.

### Every foreign-key picker is capped at 100 rows with no search, so in a school of more than 100 teachers or subjects the missing rows are unselectable and the form says so without offering a remedy. — `(school)/school/timetable/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 265
- **What a user sees:** In a school with 140 teachers, the 40 sorting after the cut-off cannot be assigned to a period at all. The form states "Showing the first 100 of 140 by first name" and offers no way to reach the rest.
- **Evidence:** Line 107 is verbatim `const OPTION_LIMIT = 100;` and line 265 is verbatim `const page = await api.page<T[]>(path, { query: { limit: OPTION_LIMIT } });`, used by all four loaders at 275-278. The truncation notices are exact: classes at 487, subjects at 668 (`… of ${subjects.total} by name.`), teachers at 722 (`… of ${teachers.total} by first name.`), sessions at 773. Grep for `type="search"` or any `<input` bound to a `q` in the file returns nothing but the two `<input type="time">` fields, so no picker has a filter. The cap is real: `config/constants.js:751-755` is `const PAGINATION = Object.freeze({ DEFAULT_PAGE: 1, DEFAULT_LIMIT: 25, MAX_LIMIT: 100 });`, enforced by `validate.js` `pagination` (`.max(PAGINATION.MAX_LIMIT)`), so raising the limit is a 422. The fix is available: `listQuery` concats `commonSchemas.search` (`search: Joi.object({ q: Joi.string().trim().allow('').max(120) })`) and `teachers.service.js:203-207` LIKEs `first_name`, `last_name` and `employee_id`, with default order `['first_name', 'ASC']` (:213).
- **Fix:** Give the teacher and subject pickers a debounced `q` bound to a small text input above the select, so the 100-row window can be aimed at the wanted name.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `OPTION_LIMIT = 100` at line 107 is the only limit sent (line 265), `MAX_LIMIT: 100` in constants.js caps it there, and there is no search input anywhere in the file — so rows past the cut-off are genuinely unreachable rather than merely hidden.

### With no `sortBy` sent, `getSort` appends an `id` tiebreaker, so within each weekday the register lists periods in creation order and there is no sort control. — `(school)/school/timetable/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — half was already fixed in the tree, the rest fixed now; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 156
- **What a user sees:** Within a weekday the rows appear in creation order, so period 7 can sit above period 1; an administrator scanning for a free period must read every row of the day, and a page boundary can split a day mid-sequence.
- **Evidence:** The query object at 157-170 sends only `page`, `limit: 20`, `q`, `day_of_week` and `is_active`, and the comment at :149-154 is verbatim, including "Sending nothing lets the route's own fallback — `['day_of_week', 'ASC']` — stand, which is the natural week." `timetable.service.js:347` is verbatim `{ where, include: INCLUDES, order: getSort({ query }, SORTABLE, ['day_of_week', 'ASC']) },`. `utils/pagination.js:62-65` is verbatim `const column = primary[primary.length - 2];` / `if (column === 'id') return [primary];` / `return [primary, ['id', primary[primary.length - 1]]];` — with the fallback, `column` is `day_of_week`, so the emitted order is `day_of_week ASC, id ASC`. The proper week order exists but is used only by the unpaginated views: `timetable.service.js:116` is `const WEEK_ORDER = Object.freeze([['day_of_week', 'ASC'], ['period_number', 'ASC']]);`, applied at :452 and :466 only.
- **Fix:** Change `list()` in `timetable.service.js:347` to fall back to `WEEK_ORDER` when no `sortBy` is requested — the same constant `classView`/`teacherView` already use. The client cannot fix it alone, since `getSort` takes one column plus the id tiebreaker.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Traced end to end: no `sortBy` in the query at 157-170, fallback `['day_of_week','ASC']` at service:347, and pagination.js:62-65 appends `['id','ASC']` because `column !== 'id'` — `period_number` never enters the ORDER BY, while WEEK_ORDER at :116 is used only at :452 and :466.

## Minor — polish, wording, and stale comments

42 finding(s) — 42 closed, 0 open (37 of them in session 28).

### The User interface — and its comment — omit the `role` object that GET /auth/me actually returns, which is why the auth screens route by isPlatform instead of by role. — `src/lib/auth.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `type-lie` · line 25
- **What a user sees:** No crash — the field is simply invisible to TypeScript. The cost is the routing defect above: login/page.tsx:51 and change-password/page.tsx:59 fall back to tenant.isPlatform, a two-way flag, to choose between five surfaces, because the field that distinguishes a parent from a principal is not declared.
- **Evidence:** auth.tsx:25 is verbatim `/** The user, as `PUBLIC_USER_FIELDS` in `auth.service.js` defines it. */` and the interface at 26-41 ends at `must_change_password: boolean;` with no `role`. But publicUser() emits more than PUBLIC_USER_FIELDS (auth.service.js:109-119 is the field list; the function then adds) — `if (user.role) { out.role = { id: user.role.id, slug: user.role.slug, name: user.role.name, isPlatformRole: Boolean(user.role.is_platform_role), isSchoolRole: Boolean(user.role.is_school_role) }; }` — and user.role is always loaded for /auth/me: authenticate.js:118 `include: [{ model: db.Role, as: 'role', attributes: ['id', 'slug', 'name', 'is_platform_role', 'is_school_role'] }],`.
- **Fix:** Add `role: { id: number; slug: string; name: string; isPlatformRole: boolean; isSchoolRole: boolean };` to the User interface and correct the comment to say the shape comes from publicUser(), not from PUBLIC_USER_FIELDS alone.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The interface and its doc comment are verbatim at 25-41, and I read publicUser() in auth.service.js: it loops PUBLIC_USER_FIELDS and then attaches `out.role` with exactly the five properties quoted. authenticate.js:118 loads the association on every authenticated request, so /auth/me always carries it. The doc comment is therefore factually wrong about the payload's shape. I am downgrading the severity from 'wrong' to 'minor': an interface describing a subset causes no runtime failure, and the whole of the stated impact is the routing defect already reported separately — this is its cause, not an independent harm.

### The client-side password-match check returns before setError(null), so a stale server error banner stays on screen beside the new field error. — `src/app/(auth)/change-password/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 48
- **What a user sees:** After a failed submit ('The new password must be different from the current one.', or a wrong current password — both arrive with no details array, so they land in the banner), the user corrects that field but mistypes the confirmation. They now see the old red banner about the already-fixed problem above a field error about a different one, and cannot tell which complaint is current.
- **Evidence:** change-password/page.tsx:48-55 is verbatim: `if (password !== confirmation) { setFieldErrors({ confirmation: 'The two passwords do not match.' }); return; }` then `setSubmitting(true); setError(null); setFieldErrors({});`. The early return at 50 precedes setError(null) at 54, so the banner rendered by line 114 `{error ? <Notice tone="error">{error}</Notice> : null}` survives. reset-password/page.tsx:38-45 has the identical ordering (`return;` at 40, `setError(null);` at 44) with its banner at line 134.
- **Fix:** Move setError(null) above the mismatch check in both files, so a locally-rejected submit clears the previous server response.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both orderings are exactly as quoted — I read change-password 40-70 and reset-password 35-64. The banner really is left standing: `error` is untouched on the early-return path and line 114 (change-password) / 134 (reset-password) still renders it. The 'different from the current one' message is real too: auth.validation.js changePassword has `password: newPassword.invalid(Joi.ref('currentPassword')).messages({ 'any.invalid': 'The new password must be different from the current one.' })`. Small, but true and reachable in two files.

### Two doc comments assert DECIMAL columns arrive as strings; `decimalNumbers: true` means they arrive as JS numbers, and both typed fields (`credit_balance`, `rate_percent`) are wrong as a result. — `frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/invoices/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — half was already fixed in the tree, the rest fixed now; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 112
- **What a user sees:** The false premise is the direct cause of the `credit_balance` money bug in the same file; the next reader of these comments inherits the belief lib/money.ts was written to kill. (The `rate_percent` consequence is cosmetic only — "12.5%" is a correct rendering of 12.5000.)
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:112-113 "`credit_balance` is `money()` — `DECIMAL(14,2)`, which Sequelize hands back as a **string**. It is displayed and never added to anything, so it stays a string here." and page.tsx:129 "/** One row of `GET /taxes`. `rate_percent` is `DECIMAL(7,4)`, so a string over the wire. */" with page.tsx:134 `rate_percent: string;`. Contradicted by config/database.js `decimalNumbers: true` and by lib/money.ts's measured note. models/billing.js:42 `rate_percent: { type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(7, 4), allowNull: false, defaultValue: 0 },`; taxes.controller.js passes the model through `ApiResponse.paginated`/`ok` unchanged.
- **Fix:** Type both as `number` and delete the string claims; format `rate_percent` explicitly if a fixed number of decimals is wanted.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `sed -n '112,113p;129p;134p'` returns those exact lines. billing.js:42 is `rate_percent: { type: DataTypes.DECIMAL(7, 4), ... }` as cited, and database.js's `decimalNumbers: true` makes both fields numbers — the session's own fixed fact is that any interface typing a money field as `string` is wrong. Confirmed as a type/doc defect; note that only the `credit_balance` half has a user-visible symptom (reported separately), so the `rate_percent` rendering claim in the impact does not add a defect.

Percent when percentage, money when fixed_amount. */` / `discount_value: string;` / `currency: string | null;` / `max_discount_amount: string | null;`. models/billing.js:69 `discount_value: money({ comment: 'Percent when percentage, currency amount when fixed_amount' }),` and :72 `max_discount_amount: money({ allowNull: true, defaultValue: null }),` — `DECIMAL(14,2)` via columns.js — returned as numbers because database.js sets `decimalNumbers: true`. coupons.controller.js `present()` is `{ ...coupon.toJSON(), remaining_uses: ... }`.

### The file header states as fact that the driver hands DECIMAL over as a string and that interpolating one prints "15.00%" — both false under this project's database config. — `frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/coupons/page.tsx`

Documentation-only: the local `formatMoney` (page.tsx:102-115) and `formatPercent` (:118-122) both start from `Number(value)`, so the rendered output is correct today.

### `PARENT_PROFILE_MISSING` and `PARENT_INACTIVE` are not in `EXPLAINED_CODES`, so both render as "Something went wrong" with a Try again button that can never succeed. — `frontend/src/app/(parent)/parent/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 151
- **What a user sees:** A parent whose parent row is missing (or whose account is deactivated) gets a red "Something went wrong" panel and a retry that cannot help; the remedy belongs to the school office and nothing on screen says so.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:81-84 `if (caught instanceof ApiError && EXPLAINED_CODES.has(caught.code)) { setRefusal(...) } else if (caught instanceof ApiError) { setError(caught.message);` and page.tsx:151 `<ErrorNotice message={error} onRetry={() => setNonce((n) => n + 1)} />`; table.tsx renders `<p className="mt-4 text-sm font-semibold text-ink">Something went wrong</p>` plus a `Try again` button. `EXPLAINED_CODES` (lib/useCollection.ts) lists the guard codes plus `'STUDENT_NOT_LINKED'` and neither parent code. parents.service.js:495 `code: 'PARENT_PROFILE_MISSING',` (on `ApiError.notFound('No parent profile is linked to this account'`) and :505 `code: 'PARENT_INACTIVE',`. apiClient.ts:296 `code = body?.error?.code ?? code;` so the code does reach the check.
- **Fix:** Add `'PARENT_PROFILE_MISSING'` and `'PARENT_INACTIVE'` to `EXPLAINED_CODES`, alongside `'STUDENT_NOT_LINKED'`, which is there for the same service-raised-refusal reason.
- **Why it survived refutation:** page.tsx:81-84 and :151 are verbatim; the printed `EXPLAINED_CODES` set contains MODULE_NOT_SUBSCRIBED, FEATURE_NOT_SUBSCRIBED, SUBSCRIPTION_INACTIVE, PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED, INSUFFICIENT_PERMISSION, INSUFFICIENT_ROLE, PLATFORM_SCOPE_REQUIRED, FORBIDDEN, SCHOOL_CONTEXT_REQUIRED, MULTIPLE_SCHOOL_CONTEXT, STUDENT_NOT_LINKED — neither parent code. parents.service.js:495 and :505 are exactly where cited, and apiClient.ts:296 shows the body code populates `ApiError.code`. Caveat: only `PARENT_PROFILE_MISSING` is plainly reachable — the service's own comment above :505 calls the inactive check "Defence in depth… this should be unreachable" — so one of the two codes is a latent case rather than a live one. Adding codes to a client-side set invents no permission or table.

### The three option lookups fire for a caller the page is about to refuse, because the effect has no permission guard — the sibling create screen documents and guards this exact case. — `frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/invoices/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 214
- **What a user sees:** Any signed-in non-platform user (or one lacking invoices.manage) who opens this URL spends three requests against the pre-auth apiLimiter before the refusal renders. No visible breakage — the refusal still shows — so the cost is wasted requests and an inconsistency with the project's own documented standard.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:214 `useEffect(() => {` runs unconditionally and issues `api.page('/subscriptions')` (:223), `api.get('/taxes')` (:241) and `api.get('/coupons')` (:251); the gate is a post-hooks return at page.tsx:369-370 `const isPlatform = profile?.tenant.isPlatform ?? false;` / `if (!isPlatform || !can('invoices.manage')) {`. Nothing above the page stops a non-platform caller: `(platform)/super-admin/layout.tsx` only renders `<AppShell nav={PLATFORM_NAV}>` and there is no `(platform)/layout.tsx`. coupons/new/page.tsx:254-260 has the guard and the reason: "The permission gate below is a `return` *after* the hooks, so without this the two lists would still be fetched for a caller who is about to be told no." then `if (!can('coupons.manage')) return;`
- **Fix:** Open the effect with `if (!(profile?.tenant.isPlatform ?? false) || !can('invoices.manage')) return;` and add `can`/`profile` to the dependency array, matching coupons/new.
- **Why it survived refutation:** `sed -n '214p;369,370p'` prints exactly `useEffect(() => {` and the two gate lines, and the effect body (200-260) issues the three calls with no permission check. I checked for an earlier gate that would make this unreachable: `find src/app -name layout.tsx` shows no `(platform)/layout.tsx`, and `(platform)/super-admin/layout.tsx` is four lines returning `<AppShell nav={PLATFORM_NAV}>{children}</AppShell>` with no scope check — so the page does mount and the requests do fire. coupons/new/page.tsx:254-260 carries the quoted comment and `if (!can('coupons.manage')) return;` verbatim.

### The Code field's hint omits two rules the schema enforces, so inputs it describes as valid are rejected with a 422. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/organizations/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 171
- **What a user sees:** A user who types `-ACME` or `_A` — both described as allowed by the hint — fills in the form, submits, and gets a validation error under the field. Small, but the hint exists precisely to prevent that round trip.
- **Evidence:** organizations/new/page.tsx:164-172 is verbatim, ending `hint="Letters, digits, hyphens and underscores. Stored in upper case."`. organizations.validation.js:61 is verbatim `const CODE_PATTERN = /^[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9_-]*$/;` and the field is verbatim `code: Joi.string().trim().uppercase().min(2).max(40).pattern(CODE_PATTERN).messages({ 'string.pattern.base': '"code" must start with a letter or digit and may contain only letters, digits, hyphens and underscores' })`. So a leading hyphen or underscore is refused, and so is a single character.
- **Fix:** State the two missing rules: 'Starts with a letter or digit, then letters, digits, hyphens and underscores. At least 2 characters. Stored in upper case.'
- **Why it survived refutation:** The hint and the Joi chain are both verbatim, and the anchored `^[A-Z0-9]` plus `.min(2)` genuinely reject inputs the hint's wording permits.

### The payments ledger surfaces per-row refundability from the API but offers no refund control, and no refunds screen exists anywhere in the app. — `E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/payments/page.tsx`

- **What a user sees:** An approved payment that needs reversing has no path in the product; the reviewer sees 'approved' and there is nothing to click, so the refund is issued out of band.
- **Evidence:** payments.controller.js `present()` ends verbatim `return { ...rest, has_screenshot: Boolean(screenshotPath), refundable_amount: refundable, is_refundable: money.toMinor(refundable) > 0, };`, and payments.routes.js:180-186 mounts `router.post('/:id/refunds', platformOnly('refunds.manage'), ...)` where `platformOnly` is `[requirePlatformScope(), requirePermission(permission)]`. The `Payment` interface at payments/page.tsx:81-95 declares neither field, and the columns array at 171-267 appends only the `review` action, gated `row.status === 'pending'`. `grep -rni refund frontend/src` returns only status-string literals and comments — no refund call anywhere — and there is no refunds directory under (platform)/super-admin.
- **Fix:** Add a Refund action on approved rows (a ConfirmDialog capturing amount and reason against `POST /payments/${id}/refunds`, gated on `can('refunds.manage')`), or build the Refunds screen. Type `refundable_amount` and `is_refundable` on the interface. Both keys already exist — nothing new is introduced.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The controller's decoration and the `refunds.manage` route are verbatim as quoted, and a case-insensitive grep for 'refund' across the entire frontend turns up no call site and no screen.

The row is built by principals.service.js present() → authService.publicUser(), which copies each PUBLIC_USER_FIELDS entry whenever it is not undefined (auth.service.js:116-118) and applies no attribute restriction on the User query (principals.service.js:185-194), so `username` is always a non-empty string on a list row. principals.validation.js:71 `username: fields.username.required(),` closes the create path.

### The header claims fieldErrors() throws on the 409's object-shaped details and that apiClient.ts was left alone, but the client already normalises details to an array, so the local guard is dead. — `(platform)/super-admin/schools/new/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `stale-comment` · line 41
- **What a user sees:** None at runtime; the next maintainer is told the shared client is unsafe when it is not, and keeps copying a redundant guard into new forms.
- **Evidence:** schools/new/page.tsx:38-46 verbatim: "Calling `fieldErrors()` on that throws `TypeError: … is not iterable` from inside this catch block, so the rejection escapes, `saving` is never cleared, and the button sticks on \"Creating…\" with nothing on screen" and "The `Array.isArray` guard below is deliberately local to this page rather than a fix in `apiClient.ts`: that client is shared by thirty screens and is not this change's to alter." apiClient.ts:104-111 is that fix: `this.details = Array.isArray(details) ? (details as unknown[]).filter((d): d is FieldError => ...) : [];`.
- **Fix:** Rewrite both headers to point at ApiError's normalisation and drop the per-page Array.isArray guards.
- **Why it survived refutation:** ApiError normalises in its constructor, and its own comment at apiClient.ts:86-103 describes having fixed precisely the failure the page header still predicts ("`fieldErrors()` then ran `for…of` over it and threw **\"details is not iterable\"** — *inside the catch block of every form*"). So `caught.details` is always FieldError[], the guard at schools/new:191 `Array.isArray(caught.details) ? caught.fieldErrors() : {}` is always true, and the sticky-"Creating…" hang is unreachable. principals/new/page.tsx:38-48 carries the same stale claim with the twin guard at :220.

### loading starts false while a fetch is guaranteed, so the first paint shows the "nothing was returned" empty state instead of a loading state. — `(platform)/super-admin/reports/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `missing-state` · line 274
- **What a user sees:** Opening Reports paints "Nothing was returned for this report." before the Subscription report arrives — the one message that tells the operator their data is missing is shown every time it is not.
- **Evidence:** reports/page.tsx:269-270: `const [report, setReport] = useState<AnyReport | null>(null);` / `const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);`. The default tab is subscription (:261) with needsSchool false and no params, so `missing` is empty (316-323) and the effect will fetch — but setLoading(true) is inside that effect (:346), after the first commit. The render chain therefore reaches 632-633 `) : !report ? (<EmptyNotice>Nothing was returned for this report.</EmptyNotice>`.
- **Fix:** Initialise loading to true, as useCollection does, or render the loading branch whenever a fetch is pending.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The state and effect lines are verbatim, and shell.tsx:190-205 shows AppShell renders no children until the session resolves (`if (loading) return …splash…` / `if (!profile) return null`), so `can('reports.subscription.view')` is already true on this component's first render — `allowed` is true, `missing` is empty, and the empty-state branch is the one that commits before the effect runs. It is a one-frame flash for a fast response rather than a persistent state, which matches the reported "minor".

### The loading state is a bare paragraph with no live region, so nothing is announced when the list is fetching; LoadingBlock exists for this. — `(platform)/super-admin/schools/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed**
- **Category:** `a11y` · line 136
- **What a user sees:** A screen-reader user gets no notification that the list is loading, and sighted users get a one-line-to-full-table jump instead of the skeleton the rest of the design system uses.
- **Evidence:** schools/page.tsx:135-136: `      ) : loading ? (` / `        <p className="text-sm text-muted">Loading…</p>`, repeated at principals/page.tsx:242-243 and reports/page.tsx:630-631. components/table.tsx:132-135 already provides the announced version: `export function LoadingBlock({ label = 'Loading…', rows = 5 }: { label?: string; rows?: number }) {` / `  return (` / `    <div className="surface overflow-hidden" aria-busy="true" aria-live="polite">` / `      <p className="sr-only">{label}</p>`.
- **Fix:** Use <LoadingBlock /> on all three screens.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both quotes are verbatim (LoadingBlock at 132-135, the finding's 131-134 being one line off), and the shared primitive is imported by none of the three screens. Half the stated impact is overstated: swapping in LoadingBlock announces the start of loading, but removing a live region does not announce completion, so "or that it has finished" would remain true after the fix.

### The primary action is a raw anchor, so it does a full document load and destroys the in-memory access token. — `(platform)/super-admin/subscriptions/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — found already fixed when re-checked against the tree; the status line had not been updated
- **Category:** `ux` · line 306
- **What a user sees:** Clicking the button reboots the SPA — blank shell, a refresh-token round trip, then the form — instead of a client transition.
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:306-312 `<a href="/super-admin/subscriptions/new" className="btn btn-primary">New subscription</a>`; the token is module state (apiClient.ts:153).
- **Fix:** Use `<Link href=…>` from `next/link`, as settings/page.tsx already does.
- **Why it survived refutation:** The anchor is verbatim at 306-312 (href on 307, className on 308, label on 310) inside the `can('subscriptions.manage')` gate, and Next does not intercept a plain `<a>`. apiClient.ts:153 is exactly `let accessToken: string | null = null;` under the comment at 147-152 beginning "In memory, never in `localStorage`", so a document load does discard it; shell.tsx:155 (cited 156) is `if (!loading && !profile) router.replace('/login');`, and auth.tsx:81 confirms the recovery path ("True until the first refresh attempt settles, so a guard does not redirect mid-recovery") — a reload, not a logout, which is what the finding describes. settings/page.tsx:33 does import `Link from 'next/link'` and uses it at :87. One correction of scope rather than fact: this is house-wide, not local — 20 list screens use a raw anchor for `btn btn-primary` (schools:111, plans:238, students:303, …) and only six files import next/link, so the fix belongs everywhere or nowhere.

Backend: users.routes.js:84-89 is `router.get('/:id', requirePermission('users.view'), validate({ params: schemas.idParam }), asyncHandler(controller.show));` whose handler is controller.js:27 `return ApiResponse.ok(res, { user: await service.presentWithPermissions(user) });`, and :91-97 is the PATCH under `requirePermission('users.manage')`, with users.validation.js:96-97 exactly "/** FR-AUTH-007 — the account status this screen's actors control. */ status: Joi.string().valid(...Object.values(USER_STATUS)),". ### The class picker silently truncates at 100 classes with no notice, unlike the two create forms that report the same cap. — `(school)/school/classes/sections/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 172
- **What a user sees:** A large school with more than 100 classes across several academic sessions finds some classes simply absent from the picker with no explanation, and their sections unreachable.

- **Fix:** Read `classes.meta?.total` and render the same "first N of M" sentence the sibling screens use, or add a session filter above the picker.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Line 172 is verbatim, and `meta` appears nowhere else in the file — the screen uses only `classes.rows`, `classes.loading`, `classes.error`, `classes.refusal` and `classes.reload`. 100 really is the ceiling: constants.js:754 `MAX_LIMIT: 100,` and pagination.js:23 `limit = Math.min(limit, PAGINATION.MAX_LIMIT);`. The sibling contrast is exact — classes/new/page.tsx:383-385 is verbatim `{sessions.state === 'ready' && sessions.total > sessions.rows.length ? ` Showing the first ${sessions.rows.length} of ${sessions.total}, newest first.` : ''}` and exams/new/page.tsx:416-419 renders "The first {classes.rows.length} of {classes.total} classes. A page cannot hold more." The premise that classes accumulate across sessions is the sibling list screen's own: classes/page.tsx's header says "a class is unique per `(school_id, academic_session_id, name)` ... and this list returns every session's classes interleaved". Beyond 100 the class's sections are unreachable, which is the "unreachable" defect class, correctly rated minor for rarity.

### A non-numeric ?class= in the URL produces a request to /classes/NaN/sections and an error banner whose Retry can never succeed. — `(school)/school/classes/sections/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)
- **Category:** `ux` · line 221
- **What a user sees:** A stale or hand-edited bookmark shows a red error with a "Try again" button that fails identically forever, and the picker above reads "Choose a class…" because no option matches.
- **Evidence:** sections/page.tsx:170 `const selected = params.get('class');` and 221 `<SectionsPanel classId={Number(selected)} />`, which builds the path unguarded at 96-99 `api.get<{ sections: SectionRow[] }>(`/classes/${classId}/sections`, ...)`.
- **Fix:** Parse once and guard on `Number.isInteger(classId) && classId > 0` before rendering the panel.
- **Why it survived refutation:** Both quotes are verbatim, and the failure path is exactly as traced: `VALIDATION_ERROR` is absent from EXPLAINED_CODES (useCollection.ts:75-100 lists only the entitlement, authorize and STUDENT_NOT_LINKED codes), so the panel's catch takes `else if (caught instanceof ApiError) { setError(caught.message); }` at 107-108 and renders `<ErrorNotice message={error} onRetry={reload} />` at 146, whose reload is `setAttempt((n) => n + 1)` against the same unchanged `classId`. Reachability is not theoretical: the file's own header states the design intent — "The chosen class lives in the URL (`?class=7`), so the screen is linkable and survives a reload" — so hand-typed and shared links are an invited entry path, and the same dead-retry occurs for a valid-looking id of a deleted class. Correctly rated minor.

- **Evidence:** library/page.tsx:62 `  fine_amount: string | number | null;`, :65 `  currency: string | null;`, and :221-225 `return (<span className="font-medium">{amount.toFixed(2)} {row.currency ?? ''}</span>);`. models/other.js:404 is `fine_amount: money({ defaultValue: 0 })` — DECIMAL(14,2) `allowNull: false` per columns.js:60-62 — and other.js:407 `currency: { type: DataTypes.STRING(10), allowNull: false, defaultValue: 'USD' }`. lib/money.ts:28-30 states "Formatting is the only thing done here", and formatAmountWithCode (:58-65) returns `1,200.00 USD` from a pinned en-US grouped formatter (:38-41).
- **Fix:** Type them as the columns are — `fine_amount: number; currency: string;` — and render `formatAmountWithCode(row.fine_outstanding, row.currency)`.

The "Formatting is the only thing done here" sentence spans 28-30 rather than the cited 29-30.

### `limits.slice(0, 8)` silently drops the ninth limit the API sends, so a school's purchased SMS credit balance can never appear on the dashboard. — `(school)/school/page.tsx`

- **Status:** **Closed** in session 28 — fixed; reviewed against the backend it calls, type-checked and linted; not reproduced in a browser (signing in needs a password this session does not enter)

- **What a user sees:** A school that has bought SMS credits sees no balance on its dashboard, and nothing on screen suggests a card was withheld — the heading just says "Limits".
- **Evidence:** page.tsx:82-84 `<dl className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-3 lg:grid-cols-4">` / `{limits.slice(0, 8).map(([key, limit]) => (` / `<MetricCard key={key} label={key.replace(/_/g, ' ')} value={formatLimit(limit)} />`. Every snapshot carries nine keys: constants.js:204 `const USAGE_LIMIT_KEYS = Object.freeze([...LIMIT_LIST, ...ADDON_ONLY_LIMITS]);` with LIMIT_LIST the eight §11.2 limits (175-186) and `ADDON_ONLY_LIMITS = Object.freeze(['sms_limit'])` (:198). emptyLimits (entitlementService.js:229-246) iterates USAGE_LIMIT_KEYS and both the unsubscribed path (:270) and the resolved path (:378) start from it, so `sms_limit` is always present and always last in insertion order. constants.js:216 labels it `'SMS Credits'` and 188-197 explains that `subscription_addons` can grant units against it.

USAGE_LIMIT_KEYS at constants.js:204, ADDON_ONLY_LIMITS at :198, LIMIT_LIST at :186 and the 'SMS Credits' label at :216 are all exact, and emptyLimits at 229-246 inserts sms_limit last, so Object.entries puts it at index 8 — the one slice(0,8) removes. It is not a dead key: entitlementService.js:437-439 does `limit.addonUnits += granted;` then `limit.value = (limit.baseValue || 0) + limit.addonUnits;`, and :645 notes an add-on "legitimately targets `sms_limit`". Only the resolved-path `const limits = emptyLimits();` is off by one (378, not 377).

The token lives in module scope — lib/apiClient.ts:153 `let accessToken: string | null = null;` — so a document navigation resets it and the next request takes the 401-then-refresh path at lib/apiClient.ts:379-384. The shell remounts and shows its loading branch (components/shell.tsx:190 `if (loading) {`) while `/auth/me` refetches. `Link` is the pattern next door: (school)/school/page.tsx:10 and :113.

The consequences check out exactly: apiClient.ts:153 is verbatim, its own header at :148-151 says "a page reload recovers the session by refreshing rather than by having stored anything sensitive", the 401 branch at :379-384 is verbatim, lib/auth.tsx:95 starts `const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);` and refetches `/auth/me` at :106, and components/shell.tsx:190 is exactly `if (loading) {`. This is behaviour, not styling.
