## Refuted — recorded so they are not re-raised

30 finding(s) did not survive. Kept because "we already checked that" is worth being able to
prove, and because a refutation can itself be wrong.

- **`src/app/(auth)/change-password/page.tsx`** — The screen has no session guard at all: it renders a complete password form to a signed-out visitor and has no loading state.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are accurate, but the load-bearing half of the impact is false. After the bootstrap's `catch { clear(); }` at auth.tsx:140-141, the form still works: request() (apiClient.ts:348-357) retries once on 401 — the submit 401s with no bearer, refreshAccessToken() runs (its ensureCsrfToken re-fetches, because clear() had just called clearCsrfToken(), so cookie and header match), the httpOnly refresh cookie is still valid, a new access token is set and send() is retried; POST /auth/change-password carries no requireCsrfToken() (auth.routes.js mounts it only on /refresh and /logout), so the retry succeeds. The reload case self-heals rather than stranding the form. What remains is a signed-out visitor who types or bookmarks /change-password — a URL nothing in the product links to — and is shown the server's own authentication message. That is not a state a user reaches in any real flow, and no data is exposed by the empty form.
- **`src/app/(auth)/verify-email/page.tsx`** — The outcome of the automatic verification POST is never announced and focus is never moved: the live region is created at the same moment as its content.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are accurate, but the premise is not. The replacement content is not an unlabelled div: Notice sets `role={tone === 'error' ? 'alert' : 'status'}` at form.tsx:324 — I read it — which is the documented ARIA technique for status messages (ARIA22), so every outcome on all three screens IS programmatically exposed as a live region. The finding rests on an implementation caveat ('a live region has to be in the DOM before its contents change to be announced reliably') that is neither a spec requirement nor measured here; inserting a role="alert" node is in fact the canonical way alerts are raised. On verify-email specifically the user arrives from an email link and has focused nothing, so 'focus falls back to <body>' describes the state they started in, not a regression. What is left is a preference for a persistent live region over a swapped one — a refinement, not a defect.
- **`frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/coupons/new/page.tsx`** — The restriction pickers are native `<select multiple>` listboxes whose hint never says how to pick more than one, so a plain second click silently discards the first selection.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are real (lines 188-197 and 161 are verbatim), but the described impact does not follow. The control is rendered as an open listbox — `size={Math.min(Math.max(rows.length, 3), 8)}` shows three to eight rows at once — and it is a controlled multi-select (`value={selected.map(String)}`), so the current selection is visibly highlighted in the box at all times; a replaced selection is shown on screen the moment it happens, not silent. Native `<select multiple>` also stays operable by keyboard (Ctrl/Shift + arrows/space) and by tap on touch, and the picker already prints its own hint plus a truncation notice. What is left is a preference for a checkbox list over a platform-standard listbox — taste about an affordance, not a defect that misreports data or blocks a task.
- **`E:/School Managment System/frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/organizations/new/page.tsx`** — A successful create navigates away with no confirmation, discarding the message the API returns for exactly this purpose.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are genuine but the impact does not follow: the destination list mounts fresh at page 1 with no filters and the backend fallback sort is `['created_at', 'DESC']` (utils/pagination.js:35), so the new organization is the top row — the described 'not visible' and 'cannot distinguish from a form reset' outcomes are unreachable.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/page.tsx`** — The whole plans surface is read-only: a plan can be created but never edited, priced, activated, archived or duplicated.
  - Refuted because: The premise that this is a plans-specific break is false. `find . -type d -name "[*]"` under frontend/src/app returns NOTHING — there is not one dynamic route anywhere in the application, so no resource has a detail screen. Plans is not an outlier; it is the uniform state of the build, which modules/page.tsx:18-20 records in the file itself: 'Read-only for now: `PUT /plans/{id}/modules` replaces the whole set… That is the next increment'. The evidence also overstates its own grep: it claims plan API calls are 'only' four call sites, but subscriptions/new/page.tsx:189 `api.get<PlanOption[]>('/plans', { query: { limit: OPTION_LIMIT, status: 'active' } })` and :235 `api.get<{ plan: PlanDetail | null }>(\`/plans/${values.plan_id}\`)` are two more. This is a request to build five new screens, not a defect in the five audited files.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/limits/page.tsx`** — The overage rate is a DECIMAL(14,2) money value rendered with a bare `toFixed(2)` — no currency, no grouping, bypassing lib/money.ts.
  - Refuted because: The quotations hold but the defect does not follow. This is not the bug class lib/money.ts exists to kill: `price.toFixed(2)` always emits exactly two decimals, so no cents are dropped — the failure money.ts documents is `String(value).split('.')` (money.ts:11), which is not what this line does. On currency, the finding's own evidence defeats its fix: plan_limits genuinely has no currency column, and a plan may hold several plan_prices rows in different currencies, so printing the code from `prices.find(p => p.is_active && p.is_default)` would attribute to the overage rate a currency the row does not carry and the plan does not uniquely define — inventing information rather than reporting it. What survives is thousands grouping on a per-unit rate, which is a cosmetic preference, not a defect.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/modules/page.tsx`** — A module with no `plan_modules` row is badged 'Draft', a state that does not exist in the data model.
  - Refuted because: The argument disproves itself. 'plan_modules has no status column at all' is equally true of 'active' and 'inactive' — the whole badge vocabulary is a UI mapping over one boolean plus the absence of a row, so 'Draft' is no more invented than the two labels the finding accepts. Its stated impact is also false on its own evidence: it says the operator 'sees three tones of pill', but components/table.tsx:373-376 puts BOTH 'inactive' and 'draft' in ENDED, so they render the identical `border-border bg-surface-2 text-muted` pill and only the word differs — which is precisely the distinction the comment says the badge exists to make, and it is present. What is left is a preference between the words 'Draft' and 'not configured'.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/modules/page.tsx`** — The orphaned-module warning banner uses raw `border-amber-300`/`text-amber-900`, roughly 1.9:1 on the dark ground.
  - Refuted because: No user can reach this. The banner renders only when `orphans.length > 0`, i.e. a `plan_modules` row whose `module_key` is not in the catalogue — and both sides read the same constant. plans.service.js:236 builds the catalogue as `modules: MODULE_LIST.map((key) => ({ key, label: MODULE_LABELS[key] }))`, while the column that would have to disagree is subscription.js:186-189 `module_key: { type: DataTypes.STRING(40), allowNull: false, validate: { isIn: [MODULE_LIST] }, ... }` — the identical `MODULE_LIST` (constants.js:148), enforced on every write, with `plans.validation` checking it again at the edge. The finding concedes the state 'should be impossible' and then rests its impact on nobody noticing the invisible banner 'until it fires'. Per the rule that a concern no user can reach is refuted, this is refuted; there is no path through the application that renders the element.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/limits/page.tsx`** — `overageAmount` is typed `string | number | null` for a DECIMAL column that always arrives as a number, forcing a defensive `Number()` coercion.
  - Refuted because: The finding disqualifies itself: its own userImpact says there is no symptom and the coercion is harmless, and the audit brief asks for defects — things broken, wrong, unusable, unreachable or lying about the data. A union that is wider than the wire format is not a lie: `number` is in it, `Number(row.overageAmount)` at limits/page.tsx:108 handles both members, and `Number.isFinite` at :109 guards the result. This is the opposite of the money.ts bug class, which came from a type that was too NARROW (`string` only) and a formatter that trusted it. Nothing here teaches a reader that a DECIMAL must be a string; it only declines to assert that it cannot be.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/limits/page.tsx`** — The overage sentence interpolates the raw unit enum, producing 'per count', 'per megabytes' and 'per requests'.
  - Refuted because: The quotations hold but the impact does not. The sentence is not read in isolation: the same row's Limit column renders `<span className="block font-medium">{row.label}</span>` over `<code>{row.key}</code>` (limits/page.tsx:79-84) and its Allowance column renders the value with the same unit appended (:94-97), so 'billed at 2.00 per count' sits inside a row headed 'Student Limit / student_limit' showing '500 count'. Nothing is misstated about the billing basis; 'per megabytes' is clumsy phrasing, which the brief excludes as taste. The proposed fix is also worse than the wording: deriving a singular noun by string-slicing `row.label` ('Student Limit' → 'student') is a guess about a display string, and no singular-noun field exists in the fixed §11.2 catalogue to read instead.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/plans/new/page.tsx`** — `INPUT_CLASS` claims to match `Field`'s input styling; the hand-rolled controls are said to have taller padding and a different vertical offset than the `Field` controls beside them.
  - Refuted because: The rendered impact is the reverse of what is claimed, because of cascade layers. Tailwind is v4.3.3 (package.json:24) and globals.css opens with `@import "tailwindcss"` (:1), which imports every Tailwind rule into the theme/base/components/utilities layers; grep for `@layer` in globals.css returns no author layer at all, so `.field-input` is UNLAYERED and unlayered author styles outrank every layered utility. INPUT_CLASS includes `field-input`, so `.field-input`'s own `padding: 0.375rem var(--space-3)`, `border: 1px solid var(--border-strong)`, `border-radius: var(--radius-md)`, `width: 100%` and `transition` all win over the `px-3 py-2 rounded-md border w-full transition-colors` beside them — those utilities are inert, not conflicting, and the padding is identical to `Field`'s, not taller. The only surviving difference is `mt-1` (4px) against `Field`'s label `mb-1.5` (6px): a 2px gap, not a visible jitter. The comment is therefore substantially accurate about the outcome it claims.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/principals/page.tsx`** — The search box and status filter hardcode bg-white with text-ink, so both are near-white text on white in dark mode.
  - Refuted because: Fabricated quotation. `grep -rn "FILTER_CLASS\|bg-white" "frontend/src/app/(platform)/super-admin/"` returns NO MATCHES — neither the constant nor the literal `bg-white` appears anywhere in the audited surface, let alone at the quoted lines.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/reports/page.tsx`** — The six school reports are gated behind a school selector that a school-scoped caller can neither populate nor legitimately use, so the reports are unreachable for the actors FR-REPORT-001 names.
  - Refuted because: The described user cannot get to the screen: the Reports nav item exists only in PLATFORM_NAV (nav.ts:106) and `find src/app -name page.tsx` lists no (school)/school/reports, so nothing in the school surface links here. SRS §33 (docs/SRS-extracted.md:1576-1613) also lists "Reports" only under Super Admin — the School checklist runs Principal Dashboard through Documents with no Reports screen — so bending this §33 Super Admin screen to school-scoped callers is not a fix the source asks for.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/schools/new/page.tsx`** — The Organization and Status selects carry aria-invalid but no error association, and the required Organization field has no required indicator at all.
  - Refuted because: `grep -n required` on schools/new/page.tsx puts `required` at line 256, inside the organization_id select — exactly the "required indicator" the finding says is absent "at all", and the basis of its user impact. (noValidate at :243 suppresses native validation UI, not the required IDL/ARIA mapping.) The contrast with principals/new is real and is why that finding stands and this one does not: grep on that file shows `required` only on its five Field components, never on its selects.
- **`(platform)/super-admin/subscriptions/new/page.tsx`** — A successful create gives no confirmation and discards the server message that names the operator's required next step.
  - Refuted because: The quotations are real (page.tsx:298-301 verbatim; controller message verbatim at :115-119), but the stated user impact does not follow and the fix cannot be implemented as written. The redirect lands on page 1 of an unfiltered list whose default order is `const DEFAULT_SORT = Object.freeze(['created_at', 'DESC']);` (subscriptions.service.js:120, applied at :234), so the row just created is the top row, and that list renders `{ key: 'state', header: 'State', cell: (row) => <StatusBadge status={row.state} /> }` — the operator IS shown "pending" or "trial" immediately, so "no feedback at all" and "never told that the subscription is Pending" are both false. The server's `message` is also unreachable from any page: apiClient.ts request() ends `const body = (await response.json()) as ApiEnvelope<T>; return body.data;`, discarding `message` for all callers, so "carrying the server's own message" would require changing the shared client, not this screen. Finally the pattern is house-wide, not a defect of this file — coupons/new:333, invoices/new:319 and organizations/new:98 all `router.replace` with no toast, and `useToast` is imported by exactly one screen (payments/page.tsx:59).
- **`(platform)/super-admin/subscriptions/page.tsx`** — The comment justifies the refusal-before-error ordering with a 402 code this endpoint cannot return.
  - Refuted because: The factual claim holds — I verified it — but there is nothing here a user can reach, which is the test I was asked to apply. For the record: `SUBSCRIPTION_INACTIVE` is raised only by `assertSubscriptionUsable()` (entitlement.js:197-215) via `requireActiveSubscription()` (:226-234), a repo-wide grep mounts that in exactly one router (documents.routes.js:64 `router.use(requireActiveSubscription());`), subscriptions.routes.js imports only `asyncHandler, validate, logActivity, requirePermission, requireAnyPermission, requirePlatformScope`, the api-level chain is `api.use(authenticate, enforcePasswordChange({...}), resolveTenant, enforceTenant)` (app.js:347-352) with no entitlement gate, and app.js:467 is `api.use('/subscriptions', subscriptionRoutes);`. So the cited code cannot arrive — but the render order is correct regardless (RefusalNotice still catches INSUFFICIENT_PERMISSION and PLATFORM_SCOPE_REQUIRED, both in EXPLAINED_CODES), no branch is wrong, no output changes, and the finding concedes the fault is a reader's-eye inaccuracy in a comment. A prose nitpick with zero reachable consequence is not a defect.
- **`(school)/school/attendance/page.tsx`** — The status column's comment reports an outstanding bug in table.tsx that the same comment says was already fixed, and that the code shows is fixed.
  - Refuted because: Verified as fact, refuted as a defect: nothing a user can reach is wrong. The quotation is verbatim (265-269) and the tone lists are exactly where cited — table.tsx:363 `const GOOD = [` includes `'present'`, :367 `const ATTENTION = [` includes `'late'`, :372 `const BAD = ['rejected', 'failed', 'suspended', 'absent', 'lost', 'fail'];`, :373 `const ENDED = [` includes `'leave'` — so all four §16 words tone correctly and every badge on this screen renders as intended. The only cost is a self-contradicting paragraph in a comment; the code it describes is correct, no output is wrong, and no user of the product can encounter it. Reported for the record, not as a finding.
- **`(school)/school/attendance/page.tsx`** — `marked_at` is typed nullable but the column is NOT NULL with a default.
  - Refuted because: The column really is NOT NULL (models/attendance.js:61, one line below the cited 60) and there really is no `present()` — controller.js:27-31 is `const pagination = getPagination(req); const result = await service.listStudents(req, req.query, pagination); return ApiResponse.paginated(res, result, pagination);`. But this is the inverse of a type-lie: the interface is wider than the wire, which is the safe direction, and the finding concedes there is no wrong output. Its supporting claim is also wrong — the `—` fallback at :331 is NOT unreachable, because `formatTimestamp` returns null on the second branch too (`if (Number.isNaN(when.getTime())) return null;` at :146), so a malformed timestamp reaches it; the finding's own fix keeps that guard, which means the fallback must stay. Applying the fix would narrow a wire type for no user benefit and remove a tolerance the null branch currently provides.
- **`(school)/school/attendance/page.tsx`** — The claim that student_attendance is the only table carrying its own provenance is contradicted by the sibling table in the same model file.
  - Refuted because: The contradiction is real and I read both halves — page.tsx:209-210 "`student_attendance` is the one table in the project that carries its own provenance, so `marked_at` is the honest \"when was this written\"", and models/attendance.js:99-105 gives TeacherAttendance the identical pair, `marked_by: fk({ allowNull: true, references: { model: 'users', key: 'id' }, … })` followed by `marked_at: { type: DataTypes.DATE, allowNull: false, defaultValue: DataTypes.NOW },` (the cited 97-104 is two lines early). But this is one adjectival overreach inside a comment whose actual argument — that `marked_at` supersedes `updated_at` for this column — the finding agrees "stands on its own". No rendering, no request, no type and no user is affected; nobody can reach it. Not a defect.
- **`(school)/school/classes/sections/page.tsx`** — The "Add section" control is hidden by an early return exactly when the class has no sections — the only moment it is needed.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are real (148 `if (rows.length === 0) return <EmptyNotice>This class has no sections yet.</EmptyNotice>;` then 152 `{can('classes.manage') ? (`), but the described impact is not this line's. The affordance being hidden is the dead `<a href={`/school/classes/${classId}/sections/new`}>` of the previous finding, and its stated impact — "there is no reachable way to create a section anywhere in the application" — follows entirely from the missing route, not from the early return. Applying this finding's own fix (move the block above line 148) delivers none of the stated benefit: it would put a link that 404s in front of more users, not fewer. This is the same single defect counted twice, and the only true remedy is the route from finding 1.
- **`(school)/school/classes/page.tsx`** — The classes list has no per-row action at all — no edit, no retire, no delete, no link to the class's sections — although all three endpoints exist.
  - Refuted because: Every factual sub-claim is true, but it is not a defect of this screen — it is the application's phase boundary, uniform across every list in the codebase. `grep -rn "api\.patch|api\.delete" frontend/src` returns ZERO hits app-wide, even though lib/apiClient.ts:522-525 exports both (`patch: <T>(path, body?, options?) => request<T>(path, { ...options, method: 'PATCH', body })`). `find src/app -type d -name "edit"` and `-name "*[[]id[]]*"` both return nothing, so no entity anywhere — schools, students, teachers, plans, invoices — has an edit or delete screen. `grep -rn "ConfirmDialog" app/` returns nothing: the primitive the fix names has never been used. Singling out classes/page.tsx for a capability that 30+ sibling list screens equally lack reports an unbuilt feature as a screen defect. The sub-claim about the Sections cell not linking to `?class=` is a navigation convenience, not a defect: nav.ts:156 already routes there (`{ label: 'Sections', href: '/school/classes/sections', ... }`).
- **`(school)/school/exams/new/page.tsx`** — A created exam with no start date is redirected to the last page of a list sorted start_date DESC, with no toast and no message, so the submit looks like it did nothing.
  - Refuted because: Every quote is verbatim, but the impact does not follow and the remedy is a convention change rather than a defect fix. "No toast after create" is the app's documented exemplar behaviour, not this screen's deviation: all 18 create screens end the same way, and organizations/new/page.tsx states the reasoning in the comment above its own `router.replace('/super-admin/organizations');` — "`replace`, not `push`: the created organization is on the list behind this screen". `grep -rn "useToast" app/` returns exactly two lines, both in super-admin/payments/page.tsx, so no create screen anywhere uses it. Nor is a self-confirming list the norm: classes.service.js:85 defaults `['numeric_order', 'ASC']`, students.service.js:404 `['first_name', 'ASC']`, subjects.service.js:183 `['name', 'ASC']` — no list in the application puts the newest row first, so "the submit looks like it did nothing" is a property of the whole app's design, not of exams/new. What remains exams-specific — NULL `start_date` sorting last under DESC — only hides the row when the school already has more than the 20 rows exams/page.tsx:188 requests AND both date fields were left blank, and the duplicate-creation consequence is speculation stacked on that compound condition.
- **`(school)/school/staff/new/page.tsx`** — A successful create navigates away with no confirmation of any kind, onto a list where the new row is not necessarily visible.
  - Refuted because: The quotations are accurate but the user impact does not follow. `router.replace('/school/staff')` at :311 sits inside the `try` block after `await api.post('/staff', body)` at :309; every failure path leaves the block at `catch (caught)` on :312 and ends at `setSaving(false)` on :339 with the user still on the form and the values intact. So arriving on the staff list IS the success signal, and "no way to tell success from a silently swallowed failure" is false — a swallowed failure never navigates. What remains is that the new row may be on a later page of an alphabetical list, which is a discoverability nicety, not a defect; and it is the app-wide pattern rather than this screen's lapse — all 22 `api.post` create screens behave identically and `grep -rn useToast` finds one importer, super-admin/payments/page.tsx:59.
- **`(school)/school/students/new/page.tsx`** — A successful admission navigates away with no success feedback, onto a paginated list that may not contain the new student.
  - Refuted because: Same reasoning as the staff/new toast finding, on code I read at :433-465: `router.replace('/school/students')` at :436 is inside the `try`, and every failure branches to `catch` at :437 and stops at `setSaving(false)` on :464 without navigating — so the navigation itself distinguishes success from failure. students.service.js:404 is exactly as quoted, so the alphabetical-placement point is true, but a missing confirmation toast on a screen that already changes route is an enhancement, not something broken, wrong, unusable or untruthful about the data.
- **`(school)/school/results/page.tsx`** — `unpublished` is not in any of StatusBadge's four tone lists, so the more consequential half of the publication column renders in the fallback "unrecognised" tone.
  - Refuted because: The mechanism is real but the user impact does not follow. I read components/table.tsx:385-393: the ENDED tone is `{ pill: 'border-border bg-surface-2 text-muted', dot: 'bg-muted-soft' }` and the fallback is `{ pill: 'border-border bg-surface-2 text-ink-soft', dot: 'bg-muted' }` — identical border and identical background, differing only in text and dot shade, and the fallback's `text-ink-soft` (globals.css:132 `--ink-soft: var(--slate-700)`) is the *higher*-contrast of the two against `bg-surface-2`. No reader can perceive a "fallback" category, the badge renders the literal word "unpublished" (table.tsx:398 `{status.replace(/_/g, ' ')}`), and the green-versus-grey distinction the column exists to draw is drawn. The suggested fix would make the badge fainter, not clearer. The line-number citation for the four lists is also off by 21 (they start at :363, not :342).
- **`(school)/school/results/page.tsx`** — `student` is typed non-optional on the grounds that `ON DELETE CASCADE` makes the join impossible to miss, but `Student` is a paranoid model, so a soft delete would leave the result row with a null join and the page would throw.
  - Refuted because: Refuted on reachability, which the finding itself concedes ("No user is affected today"). I checked rather than took its word: `grep -rn "\.destroy(" backend/src/modules/` returns twenty hits and not one is a Student — the students module has none — and students.routes.js mounts only `router.post` (:62, :89, :98, :106, :114) and `router.patch` (:129), with no DELETE at all. So no code path can produce the null join, and no user can reach a TypeError. The underlying observation about `paranoid: true` is correct and the comment is imprecise, but a code-comment nicety with a self-declared zero user impact is not a defect of this screen.
- **`frontend/src/app/(school)/school/teachers/new/page.tsx`** — The Salary input states no currency, but the amount is per-school (`school_settings.currency`, default USD) and feeds expense rows that carry an explicit currency column.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are all real, but the impact does not follow and the fix's premise is false. `teachers.salary` has no currency column precisely because a school has exactly one currency, so there is no ambiguity to resolve and no glyph on the form to mislead — the money.ts principle the finding leans on is explicitly scoped away from this screen: the sentence immediately before the quoted fragment (money.ts:31) reads "Currency stays a three-letter code rather than a glyph **on the platform screens**. Those lists span every school on the platform." This is a single-tenant screen. And "it is already in the settings payload" is not true: `lib/auth.tsx`'s `Profile` interface (51-61) carries only user, permissions, tenant and entitlements; `grep -rn currency lib/` matches only money.ts's own parameters, and no `(school)` screen fetches school settings. An unlabelled amount in a school's own single currency is a polish preference, not a defect.
- **`frontend/src/app/(school)/school/students/page.tsx`** — The empty states pass neither `title` nor `action` to `EmptyNotice`, so the one moment when creating a record is the only useful thing to do offers no way to do it.
  - Refuted because: The quotes are accurate, but the stated impact is not reachable — nothing here is unavailable. On all three screens the create button and the filter controls are rendered on the same screen directly above the empty notice, in that order: students/page.tsx renders `PageHeader` with `action={can('students.manage') ? (<a href="/school/students/new" …>Admit student</a>) : null}` at 300-307, then the search and status controls at ~345-375, and only then reaches `) : rows.length === 0 ? (` at 391. So the create affordance is a few pixels above the message and the filters that produced the filtered-empty state are still sitting there to be cleared. Duplicating a visible control into the empty state is a polish preference against a component's stylistic guidance, not a defect.
- **`(school)/school/timetable/page.tsx`** — Both filter selects hard-code `bg-white` alongside the theme token `text-ink`, so in dark mode they render #f1f3f7 text on a #ffffff background.
  - Refuted because: Fabricated quotation. Lines 367 and 395 both read `className="field-select"`; the string `bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-ink` occurs nowhere in the file or in `frontend/src` at all.
- **`(student)/student/page.tsx`** — The portal tells the student their timetable is held by the school office, but the catalogue grants Student `timetable.view` and the backend mounts a class-timetable route documented as the student's way to read it.
  - Refuted because: The quotations are all real, but the impact does not follow: `STUDENT_NAV` (nav.ts:240-245) contains exactly one item, 'My results', and no student-reachable timetable screen exists anywhere in `frontend/src`, so no user is turned away by this sentence — a permission grant on the API is not product access.

## Session 28: the screens this sweep never saw

The twenty screens built after the sweep above — the detail and create screens of sessions 27 and 28,
and the three panels of the subscription detail screen — had never been read against their backend.
Five read-only agents did it, one group each, the way the first sweep did: each screen against the
routes, Joi schemas and services it calls. **132 findings.** They were treated as claims: every one
was re-read against the tree before it was acted on, and the backend half of each was fixed and
asserted here rather than papered over in the screen.

Six agents then fixed the frontend half, one group of screens each, and **three more read-only
reviewers** read every diff they produced against the backend. The reviewers found fifteen defects
the fixes had introduced or left — among them a plan-change panel that still offered prices the API
had just started refusing, an attendance register that wrote one day's marks onto the next when the
date was changed, and a reports screen that fetched every report twice. All fifteen were re-checked
by hand and fixed. The whole frontend type-checks and lints clean and `verify-frontend.js` passes; as
with the 80 closed above in the same session, **nothing here was reproduced in a browser**, because
signing in needs a password this session does not enter.

**All 132 are closed.** Where the fix was on the server, the row says so and names the suite that
now asserts it. Two rows leave a question for the product owner rather than an answer, and say which.

### Group A — platform billing: `coupons/[id]`, `payments/new`, `plans/[id]`, `quotations`, `taxes` (25)

| # | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | A coupon edit's "Reason" was silently stripped — the update schema lacked it | Backend: `coupons.validation` update accepts `reason`, and the service passes it to the audit row (`verify-billing.js`). Frontend shows its errors |
| C2 | Validity dates sent zoneless from `datetime-local` | `isoInstant` (now `lib/instants.ts`, shared with the create screen) |
| C3 | Switching to a fixed amount with no currency was accepted | Backend: `coupons.service.update()` checks the merged row (`verify-billing.js`). Frontend refuses it under Currency, and a switch to percentage sends `currency: null` |
| C4 | An expired coupon showed "Active" in its status select | A disabled "Expired (set by the system)" option, and a hint that extending the window does not reactivate it |
| C5 | Plan and school restrictions not shown or editable | Both pickers added; ids the picker cannot list are kept, not dropped |
| C6 | Copy mentioned a "used up" status that does not exist | Removed |
| PM1 | Record offered `online_gateway`, which no shipped adapter can take | Removed from Record, with the reason in the source; `gateway_failed` now shows as a failure |
| PM2 | The invoice picker stopped at 100 with no search | The total is shown and a debounced search filters by number |
| PM3 | The submit hint called the screenshot optional after D8 | Hint follows D8, and the form requires a transaction id or a screenshot (wallet excepted) |
| PM4 | No confirmation when Record overpays an invoice | Confirm dialog above the amount due, and an inline warning |
| PM5 | A failed invoice load read "Nothing is outstanding" | An error on the select with Try again |
| P1 | Saving prices re-created every row; an in-use row was kept *and* duplicated, and the next save was refused | Backend: `plans.service.setPrices()` updates a row in place by identity and retires only what the set drops (`verify-plans.js`). Frontend reads `retired`, and the Remove dialog says what happens to a price in use |
| P2 | Switching tabs unmounted the form and lost its edits | Both panels stay mounted; each re-seeds only from its own part of the plan |
| P3 | Stale comment: `status` is refused, not stripped | Corrected |
| Q1 | Accept sent `convert: true` for a quotation with no school, which is refused | A School picker; Accept sends `convert: false` when there is none, and says no invoice follows. The service header, which claimed the opposite, is corrected |
| Q2 | A lapsed "sent" quote looked live | Expired badge and no Accept. Backend: the `expireLapsed()` sweep existed and **nothing scheduled it** — `jobs/tasks/quotationExpiry.js` now runs it daily (`verify-jobs.js`) |
| Q3 | "Amount" was the line total, not a unit price | Labelled "Line total" |
| Q4 | Lines without a description were dropped silently | Only fully blank lines are dropped; a line's server errors show under it |
| Q5 | An edit could not clear a field | Cleared fields are sent as `null` (or 0 for discount and tax); only changes are sent |
| Q6 | Notes opened empty on edit | Loaded from the row |
| Q7 | Line items were not editable on a draft | Seeded from `line_items` and editable, keeping each line's type and metadata |
| Q8 | Toast said "sended" | Past-tense map |
| T1 | The create dialog's error rendered behind its backdrop | Errors inside the dialog |
| T2 | "Make default" offered on inactive taxes, always refused | Only on active taxes that are not the default |
| T3 | "A default exists" read from the current page only | Its own `?is_default=true&limit=1` query |

| # | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|

| S6 | Suspension and archive dates and reason never shown | Shown, with a hint per action |
| S7 | "Create one on the Principals screen" lost the school | Links with `?school_id=`, which preselects it and returns |
| S8–S10 | Code "unique across the platform" (it is per organization); a nonexistent archived filter; a stale comment | Corrected — the create screen's identical claim too, found by the reviewer |
| B1 | "The school has no access" on an old subscription of a school with a newer live one | "This subscription grants no entitlement in its current state" |
| B2 | "Trial ends in −3 day(s)" | "Has passed; the sweep has not moved it on yet", and "within a day" |
| B3 | Credit balance shown without its currency | Formatted with the code |
| B4 | "Per cycle" on a one-time subscription | Only when recurring |
| B5 | "Nightly sweep" — it runs hourly | Corrected in both places |
| B6 | Stale guard comment | Corrected |
| U1 | The verification toast ignored `verificationEmailSent`; the form never re-seeded | Keyed on the flag; re-seeds from the saved user |
| U2 | A blank required field was skipped silently | Sent, and refused on the field; Save disabled when nothing changed |
| U3 | Own status and permissions looked editable but are refused | Read-only on your own account, with the reason |
| U4 | The `super_admin` role matrix looked editable but is refused | Read-only |
| U5 | Role label errors read "Validation failed" | On their fields |
| U6 | Saving role permissions discarded other tabs' edits | Re-reads only the account |
| U7 | The account's school or organization never shown | In the header |
| U8–U12 | Wording, read failures blamed on permissions, sign-in rule, header counts, literal backticks | Corrected |
| F1 | The reset confirmation hid the address and offered no retry | Shows the address and "Use a different address" |
| F2 | Comment said 200; the route answers 202 | Corrected |
| X1 | A bad or missing id read "Something went wrong" | A not-found state with a way back, on all three detail screens |
| X2 | SRS section numbers in user-facing copy | Removed from the copy; kept in comments |

### Group C — school: `ai`, `assignments`, `attendance/mark`, `exams/grade-scales`, `notifications` (37)

| # | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | An AI bank stuck at `generated`: review needs `preview`, and the one call that moves it sat in an unreachable branch | A "Set the difficulty" step for `ai.approve`; review offered only at `preview` |
| C-2 | Uploading without a file did nothing visible | Required, checked, and its error shown |

| C-5 | A rejected bank showed "Pending" | Mapped |
| C-6 | Step failures read "Validation failed" and did not reload | The server's detail, and a reload |

| C-8–C-10 | "The file is checked against it" (no `accept`); "1 marks"; stale header | File types restricted; singular; header corrected |
| C-11 | The Mark dialog showed neither the answer nor the file | Title, total, written answer and a download |
| C-12 | Clearing a due date was refused as "must be a valid date" | Backend: `due_date` accepts `null` — the column is nullable (`verify-assignments.js`) |
| C-13 | Students and parents could not reach assignments or notifications | Linked from both dashboards — the parent's assignments link added after the review |
| C-14 | A submission's student read "undefined undefined" | Backend: the list includes the student's name (`verify-assignments.js`); the screen falls back to the admission number |
| C-15 | "Submit work" on work already submitted | "Submit work", "Resubmit" or "Handed in", from the student's own submissions |
| C-16 | Stale header | Corrected |
| C-17 | Clearing the scale on edit kept the old one | Sends `default` |
| C-18 | The scale filter lost names off-screen; mixed scales interleaved | Names kept across pages; sorted by scale, then band |
| C-19 | The overlap refusal did not say which band | Backend: the message names the band and its range, and the lookup is ordered so it always names the same one (`verify-exams.js`); the screen reads `conflicts_with` as well |
| C-20 | The client allowed min = max, which the model refuses | Refused |
| C-21 | A notice promised platform bands were editable | Removed; no Edit on them |
| C-22 | The hint said re-marking a day is refused — it overwrites; a section save reset the register | Hint corrected, section kept. The reviewer then found that changing the date kept the previous day's marks — now reset to everybody present, as the hint says |
| C-23 | The register's "Reason" was accepted and dropped | Backend: kept in the activity row's metadata (`verify-attendance.js`); the copy says the activity log, not an audit entry |
| C-24 | The register stopped at 100 | Read page by page to 500, with a warning past it |
| C-25–C-28 | A dropped connection was silent; `entries` errors swallowed; errors doubled in the banner; load failures mis-explained | The fallback message; `entries` out of `FORM_FIELDS` and submit disabled until the roster is ready; one place per error; each failure said as what it was |
| C-29 | Every notification's "Open" went to an API path no screen answers | `destinationFor()` maps each to its screen, and hides Open when none |
| C-30 | "Mark read" on a failed email killed its retry | In-app rows only |
| C-31 | "Mark all read" did nothing on the email view | Hidden there |
| C-32 | "All channels" showed in-app only | "Inbox (in app)" |
| C-33 | Mark all was gated on the current page | An unread count across every page |
| C-34 | Long messages were cut with no way to read them | Two lines, the whole text on hover |
| C-35 | A failure-reason line the server never fills | Removed |
| C-36 | Timestamps sliced to the UTC day | The viewer's zone |
| C-37 | Status colours backwards — "returned" green, "reviewed" amber, a sent email amber | `StatusBadge` `tone`; each corrected |

| W3 | A receptionist saw "Not recorded" for a recorded session | The stored session is offered when the list cannot show it |
| W4 | Copy claimed inactive subjects vanish from timetables and mark sheets | Reworded to what the services do |
| W5 | Failed or cut pickers showed blank for stored values | The stored value is always an option; lists read past 100 |

| P2 | Session create errors read "Validation failed" | On the name, start and end fields |
| P3 | A teacher saw an editable subject form that 403s | Read-only without `subjects.manage` |
| P4 | Assigning reloaded the page and discarded unsaved edits | Re-reads only the two lists |

| P6 | After creating a subject the screen went to the list | Goes to the subject |
| P7 | A timetable clash did not say with what | Reads `ApiError.context.with` and links to the clashing entry |

| M1–M10 | Shared dialog errors; silent no-op Save; `EditDialog` dropping an emptied required field; unmarked required fields; clearing a class sent 0; retired-entry copy; stale comments; developer vocabulary; an unenforced "http(s)" | Each corrected — `EditDialog` now refuses an emptied required field on the field, for all ten screens that use it |

### Group E — the subscription panels: plan change, add-ons, overrides (18)

| # | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| E-1 | A plan change sent only `plan_id`, so a yearly school could be moved onto a monthly price | Backend: the cycle is kept unless a price or cycle is named, and refused when the plan has none on it. The panel names a price, and lists only the ones the API accepts |
| E-2 | The prorated amount due was shown and never billed | Backend: issued as its own invoice in the same transaction, returned as `change.invoice` (`verify-subscriptions.js`); the panel names it |
| E-3 | The new price ignored an active price override (D7) | Both shown |
| E-4 | "Next billing cycle" offered on a one-time subscription, where it can never land | Backend: refused (`SUBSCRIPTION_NO_NEXT_CYCLE`, `verify-subscriptions.js`); the timing is not offered |
| E-5 | Add-on prices on another cycle or currency were offered and billed wrongly | Backend: refused (`ADDON_PRICE_CYCLE_MISMATCH`, `verify-billing.js`); the list is filtered |
| E-6 | Cancelling one of two purchases of an add-on stopped billing both | Backend: each billing line names its purchase and only that one closes (`verify-subscriptions.js`, proved against the old behaviour) |
| E-7 | A feature override key was not normalised, and a mixed-case one rewrote the lower-case row | Backend: lower-cased and pattern-checked like a plan feature key (`verify-subscriptions.js`); the form suggests known keys |
| E-8 | "Effective until" ended at 00:00 UTC | Local day bounds, labelled inclusive |
| E-9 | Re-applying a revoked target overwrote it silently | Warned, and the toast says whether it replaced a row |
| E-10 | The limit hint said an override replaces the whole allowance; add-on units stack | Corrected, with the units held |
| E-11 | The price hint overstated D7 | Plan line only |
| E-12 | 422s read "Validation failed"; submit enabled with blanks | Field errors; submit disabled until filled; reasons capped |
| E-13 | Forms shown on unusable subscriptions; "Reactivate it first" wrong for pending or paused | A notice naming the transition that brings it into use. Backend: the refusal message is per state |
| E-14 | A scheduled downgrade dropped or replaced silently | Warned |
| E-15–E-18 | "No invoice line" for a free purchase (a 0.00 line prints); the carried credit not shown; an empty dropdown on a refusal; raw keys | Each corrected |

**Found while fixing E-1, and fixed with it.** Naming another price opened two more mistakes the
audit had not reached. The proration multiplied the *new* price by the *old* period's fraction, so a
yearly school moved halfway onto a monthly price paid half of one month for six months — `prorate()`
now charges the remaining days at the new price's own daily rate (unchanged on the same cycle). And a
price in another currency carried the credit across as the same number relabelled. **The service now
refuses a plan change onto another currency, and between one-time and recurring**, since nothing in
the platform converts currency and a one-time subscription is never renewed. That refusal is a
judgement about arithmetic the SRS never describes, not a requirement — the owner may prefer to allow
a currency change with some rule for the credit, and is asked. `verify-subscriptions.js` asserts the
daily rate, the unchanged same-cycle figure and both refusals.
